“Seyyathāpi, bhante, āpasmiṃ sucimpi dhovanti asucimpi dhovanti gūthagatampi… muttagatampi… kheḷagatampi… pubbagatampi… lohitagatampi dhovanti, na ca tena āpo aṭṭīyati vā harāyati vā jigucchati vā; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, āposamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena [M 3.0182] averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

“Venerable Sir, just as people wash both clean and unclean things in water—they wash feces, they wash urine, they wash spittle, they wash pus, and they wash blood—and the water is not thereby distressed, humiliated, or disgusted; even so, Venerable Sir, I dwell with a mind like water: vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Venerable Sir, it is surely one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is unestablished who might, in this Dispensation, collide with a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a tour without making amends.

“Seyyathāpi, bhante, tejo sucimpi ḍahati asucimpi ḍahati gūthagatampi… muttagatampi… kheḷagatampi… pubbagatampi… lohitagatampi ḍahati, na ca tena tejo aṭṭīyati vā harāyati vā jigucchati vā; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ[T 4.0389], bhante, tejosamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

“Venerable Sir, just as fire burns both clean and unclean things—it burns feces, it burns urine, it burns spittle, it burns pus, and it burns blood—and the fire is not thereby distressed, humiliated, or disgusted; even so, Venerable Sir, I dwell with a mind like fire: vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Venerable Sir, it is surely one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is unestablished who might, in this Dispensation, collide with a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a tour without making amends.

“Seyyathāpi, bhante, vāyo sucimpi upavāyati asucimpi upavāyati gūthagatampi… muttagatampi… kheḷagatampi… pubbagatampi… lohitagatampi upavāyati, na ca tena vāyo aṭṭīyati vā harāyati vā jigucchati vā; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, vāyosamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati [V 3.0192] anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

Just as, venerable sir, the wind blows upon both what is clean and what is unclean—feces, urine, saliva, pus, and blood—yet the wind is not repelled, ashamed, or disgusted by it; in the same way, venerable sir, I dwell with a mind like the wind—vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.

“Seyyathāpi[P 4.0376], bhante, rajoharaṇaṃ sucimpi puñchati asucimpi puñchati gūthagatampi… muttagatampi… kheḷagatampi… pubbagatampi… lohitagatampi puñchati, na ca tena rajoharaṇaṃ aṭṭīyati vā harāyati vā jigucchati vā; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, rajoharaṇasamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

Just as, venerable sir, a dust-cloth wipes both what is clean and what is unclean—feces, urine, saliva, pus, and blood—yet the dust-cloth is not repelled, ashamed, or disgusted by it; in the same way, venerable sir, I dwell with a mind like a dust-cloth—vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.

“Seyyathāpi, bhante, caṇḍālakumārako vā caṇḍālakumārikā vā kaḷopihattho nantakavāsī gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā pavisanto nīcacittaṃyeva upaṭṭhapetvā [T 4.0390] pavisati; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, caṇḍālakumārakacaṇḍālakumārikāsamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati [M 3.0183] anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

Just as, venerable sir, a young caṇḍāla boy or a young caṇḍāla girl, with a basket in hand and wearing worn-out garments, upon entering a village or a town, does so having established a humble mind; in the same way, venerable sir, I dwell with a mind like that of a young caṇḍāla boy or a young caṇḍāla girl—vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.

“Seyyathāpi, bhante, usabho chinnavisāṇo sūrato sudanto suvinīto rathiyāya rathiyaṃ siṅghāṭakena siṅghāṭakaṃ anvāhiṇḍanto na kiñci hiṃsati pādena vā visāṇena vā; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, usabhachinnavisāṇasamena cetasā viharāmi vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

Just as, venerable sir, a bull with cut horns, gentle, well-tamed, and well-trained, wandering from street to street, from crossroad to crossroad, harms no one with its foot or horn; in the same way, venerable sir, I dwell with a mind like a bull with cut horns—vast, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.

“Seyyathāpi, bhante, itthī vā puriso vā daharo yuvā maṇḍanakajātiko sīsaṃnhāto ahikuṇapena vā kukkurakuṇapena [P 4.0377] vā manussakuṇapena vā kaṇṭhe āsattena aṭṭīyeyya harāyeyya jiguccheyya; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, iminā pūtikāyena aṭṭīyāmi harāyāmi jigucchāmi. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyya.

Just as, venerable sir, a young woman or a young man, fond of adornment, having bathed their head, would be repelled, ashamed, and disgusted if the carcass of a snake, a dog, or a human were hung on their neck; in the same way, venerable sir, I am repelled, ashamed, and disgusted by this foul body. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.

“Seyyathāpi[V 3.0193], bhante, puriso medakathālikaṃ parihareyya chiddāvachiddaṃ uggharantaṃ paggharantaṃ; evamevaṃ kho ahaṃ, bhante, imaṃ kāyaṃ pariharāmi chiddāvachiddaṃ [T 4.0391] uggharantaṃ paggharantaṃ. Yassa nūna, bhante, kāye kāyagatāsati anupaṭṭhitā assa, so idha aññataraṃ sabrahmacāriṃ āsajja appaṭinissajja cārikaṃ pakkameyyā”ti.

Just as, venerable sir, a man might carry a pot of fat that has large and small holes, oozing and dripping; in the same way, venerable sir, I carry this body, which has large and small holes, oozing and dripping. Surely, venerable sir, one in whom mindfulness directed to the body is not established would, in this Teaching, affront a certain fellow in the holy life and depart on a journey without relinquishing the fault.”

Atha kho so bhikkhu uṭṭhāyāsanā ekaṃsaṃ uttarāsaṅgaṃ karitvā bhagavato pādesu sirasā nipatitvā bhagavantaṃ etadavoca – “accayo maṃ, bhante, accagamā yathābālaṃ yathāmūḷhaṃ yathāakusalaṃ, yo ahaṃ āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ asatā tucchā musā abhūtena abbhācikkhiṃ. Tassa me, bhante, bhagavā accayaṃ accayato paṭiggaṇhatu āyatiṃ saṃvarāyā”ti. “Taggha taṃ tvaṃ (sī. pī.), bhikkhu, accayo accagamā yathābālaṃ yathāmūḷhaṃ yathāakusalaṃ, yo tvaṃ sāriputtaṃ asatā tucchā musā abhūtena abbhācikkhi. Yato ca kho tvaṃ, bhikkhu, accayaṃ accayato disvā yathādhammaṃ [M 3.0184] paṭikarosi, taṃ te mayaṃ paṭiggaṇhāma. Vuḍḍhihesā, bhikkhu, ariyassa vinaye yo accayaṃ accayato disvā yathādhammaṃ paṭikaroti āyatiṃ saṃvaraṃ āpajjatī”ti.

Then that monk rose from his seat, arranged his upper robe over one shoulder, prostrated with his head at the Blessed One's feet, and said this to the Blessed One: “Venerable sir, a transgression overcame me, according to my foolishness, my delusion, and my unskillfulness, in that I accused the Venerable Sāriputta with what was non-existent, empty, false, and untrue. Venerable sir, may the Blessed One accept my transgression as a transgression for the sake of restraint in the future.” “Indeed, monk, a transgression overcame you, according to your foolishness, your delusion, and your unskillfulness, in that you accused Sāriputta with what was non-existent, empty, false, and untrue. But since you, monk, having seen the transgression as a transgression, make amends in accordance with the Dhamma, we accept it from you. For this is growth in the discipline of the Noble One, monk: that one who, having seen a transgression as a transgression, makes amends in accordance with the Dhamma, attains restraint in the future.”

Atha [P 4.0378] kho bhagavā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ āmantesi – “khama, sāriputta, imassa moghapurisassa, purā tassa tattheva sattadhā muddhā phalatī”ti phalissatīti (ka. sī. syā. pī. ka.) aṭṭhakathāsu pana “phalatīti” itveva dissati. “Khamāmahaṃ, bhante, tassa āyasmato sace maṃ so āyasmā evamāha – ‘khamatu ca me so āyasmā’”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

Then the Blessed One addressed the Venerable Sāriputta: “Forgive, Sāriputta, this worthless man, before his head splits into seven pieces right here.” “Venerable sir, I forgive that venerable one, and may that venerable one also forgive me.” The first discourse is finished.

2. Saupādisesasuttaṃ

2. The Discourse with Remainder

12. Ekaṃ samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya [T 4.0392] sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisi. Atha kho āyasmato sāriputtassa etadahosi – “atippago kho tāva sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya carituṃ, yaṃnūnāhaṃ yena aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ ārāmo tenupasaṅkameyya”nti. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto yena aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ ārāmo tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā tehi aññatitthiyehi paribbājakehi saddhiṃ sammodi. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi.

12. At one time the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s monastery. Then, in the morning, the Venerable Sāriputta dressed, took his bowl and robe, and entered Sāvatthī for alms. Then this thought occurred to the Venerable Sāriputta: “It is still too early to go for alms in Sāvatthī. What if I were to approach the park of the wanderers of other persuasions?” Then the Venerable Sāriputta approached the park of the wanderers of other persuasions. Having approached, he exchanged greetings with those wanderers of other persuasions. When they had concluded their courteous and memorable talk, he sat down to one side.

Tena [V 3.0194] kho pana samayena tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – “yo hi koci, āvuso, saupādiseso kālaṃ karoti, sabbo so aparimutto nirayā aparimutto tiracchānayoniyā aparimutto pettivisayā aparimutto apāyaduggativinipātā”ti. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ bhāsitaṃ neva abhinandi nappaṭikkosi. Anabhinanditvā appaṭikkositvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkāmi – “bhagavato santike etassa bhāsitassa atthaṃ ājānissāmī”ti. Atha kho āyasmā sāriputto sāvatthiyaṃ piṇḍāya caritvā pacchābhattaṃ [P 4.0379] piṇḍapātapaṭikkanto yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā [M 3.0185] ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā sāriputto bhagavantaṃ etadavoca –

At that time, this conversation arose among those wanderers of other persuasions who were sitting and assembled together: “Friends, whoever passes away with a remainder, all such persons are not liberated from hell, not liberated from the animal realm, not liberated from the sphere of afflicted spirits, not liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.” Then, the Venerable Sāriputta neither delighted in nor rejected the statement of those wanderers of other persuasions. Without delighting in or rejecting it, he rose from his seat and departed, thinking: “I shall learn the meaning of this statement in the presence of the Blessed One.” Then, the Venerable Sāriputta, having gone for alms in Sāvatthī and returned from his alms round after his meal, approached the Blessed One. Having approached, he paid homage to the Blessed One and sat down to one side. Sitting to one side, the Venerable Sāriputta said this to the Blessed One:

“Idhāhaṃ, bhante, pubbaṇhasamayaṃ nivāsetvā pattacīvaramādāya sāvatthiṃ piṇḍāya pāvisiṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, bhante, etadahosi – ‘atippago kho tāva sāvatthiyaṃ [T 4.0393] piṇḍāya carituṃ; yaṃnūnāhaṃ yena aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ ārāmo tenupasaṅkameyya’nti. Atha kho ahaṃ, bhante, yena aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ ārāmo tenupasaṅkamiṃ; upasaṅkamitvā tehi aññatitthiyehi paribbājakehi saddhiṃ sammodiṃ. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdiṃ. Tena kho pana samayena tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ sannisinnānaṃ sannipatitānaṃ ayamantarākathā udapādi – ‘yo hi koci, āvuso, saupādiseso kālaṃ karoti, sabbo so aparimutto nirayā aparimutto tiracchānayoniyā aparimutto pettivisayā aparimutto apāyaduggativinipātā’ti. Atha kho ahaṃ, bhante, tesaṃ aññatitthiyānaṃ paribbājakānaṃ bhāsitaṃ neva abhinandiṃ nappaṭikkosiṃ. Anabhinanditvā appaṭikkositvā uṭṭhāyāsanā pakkamiṃ – ‘bhagavato santike etassa bhāsitassa atthaṃ ājānissāmī’”ti.

“Venerable Sir, today in the morning, having dressed, taken my bowl and robe, I entered Sāvatthī for alms. Then, Venerable Sir, this thought occurred to me: ‘It is still too early to go for alms in Sāvatthī; what if I were to approach the park of the wanderers of other persuasions?’ Then, Venerable Sir, I approached the park of the wanderers of other persuasions. Having approached, I exchanged greetings with those wanderers of other persuasions. When we had concluded our courteous and memorable talk, I sat down to one side. At that time, this conversation arose among those wanderers of other persuasions who were sitting and assembled together: ‘Friends, whoever passes away with a remainder, all such persons are not liberated from hell, not liberated from the animal realm, not liberated from the sphere of afflicted spirits, not liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.’ Then, Venerable Sir, I neither delighted in nor rejected the statement of those wanderers of other persuasions. Without delighting in or rejecting it, I rose from my seat and departed, thinking: ‘I shall learn the meaning of this statement in the presence of the Blessed One.’”

“Ke ca keci (syā. pī.), te ca (ka.), sāriputta, aññatitthiyā paribbājakā bālā abyattā, ke ca keci (syā. pī. ka.) a. ni. 6.44 pāḷiyā saṃsandetabbaṃ saupādisesaṃ vā ‘saupādiseso’ti jānissanti, anupādisesaṃ vā ‘anupādiseso’ti jānissanti”!

“Sāriputta, who are these wanderers of other persuasions, foolish and unskilled, that they would know one with a remainder as ‘one with a remainder,’ or one without a remainder as ‘one without a remainder’?”

“Navayime, sāriputta, puggalā saupādisesā kālaṃ kurumānā parimuttā nirayā parimuttā tiracchānayoniyā parimuttā pettivisayā parimuttā apāyaduggativinipātā. Katame nava? Idha[P 4.0380], sāriputta, ekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ paripūrakārī[V 3.0195], paññāya mattaso kārī. So pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā antarāparinibbāyī hoti. Ayaṃ, sāriputta[T 4.0394], paṭhamo puggalo saupādiseso kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā parimutto tiracchānayoniyā [M 3.0186] parimutto pettivisayā parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

“Sāriputta, these nine types of individuals, passing away with a remainder, are liberated from hell, liberated from the animal realm, liberated from the sphere of afflicted spirits, liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin. Which nine? Here, Sāriputta, a certain individual is one who fulfills virtue and fulfills concentration, but practices wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the five lower fetters, he is one who attains Parinibbāna in the interval. This, Sāriputta, is the first individual who, passing away with a remainder, is liberated from hell, liberated from the animal realm, liberated from the sphere of afflicted spirits, liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.

“Puna caparaṃ, sāriputta, idhekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ paripūrakārī, paññāya mattaso kārī. So pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā upahaccaparinibbāyī hoti…pe… asaṅkhāraparinibbāyī hoti…pe… sasaṅkhāraparinibbāyī hoti…pe… uddhaṃsoto hoti akaniṭṭhagāmī. Ayaṃ, sāriputta, pañcamo puggalo saupādiseso kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā parimutto tiracchānayoniyā parimutto pettivisayā parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

“Furthermore, Sāriputta, here a certain individual is one who fulfills virtue and fulfills concentration, but practices wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the five lower fetters, he is one who attains Parinibbāna upon landing; … or one who attains Parinibbāna without exertion; … or one who attains Parinibbāna with exertion; … or one who goes upstream, heading for the Akaniṭṭha realm. This, Sāriputta, is the fifth individual who, passing away with a remainder, is liberated from hell, liberated from the animal realm, liberated from the sphere of afflicted spirits, liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.

“Puna caparaṃ, sāriputta, idhekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ mattaso kārī, paññāya mattaso kārī. So tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā rāgadosamohānaṃ tanuttā sakadāgāmī hoti, sakideva imaṃ lokaṃ āgantvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ, sāriputta, chaṭṭho puggalo saupādiseso kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā…pe… parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

“Furthermore, Sāriputta, here a certain individual is one who fulfills virtue, but practices concentration and wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the three fetters and the attenuation of greed, aversion, and delusion, he is a once-returner, who, having come to this world only once, makes an end of suffering. This, Sāriputta, is the sixth individual who, passing away with a remainder, is liberated from hell… is liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.

“Puna caparaṃ, sāriputta, idhekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ mattaso kārī, paññāya mattaso kārī. So tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā ekabījī hoti, ekaṃyeva mānusakaṃ bhavaṃ nibbattetvā dukkhassantaṃ [P 4.0381] karoti. Ayaṃ, sāriputta, sattamo puggalo saupādiseso [T 4.0395] kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā…pe… parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

“Furthermore, Sāriputta, here a certain individual is one who fulfills virtue, but practices concentration and wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the three fetters, he becomes a ‘one-seeder’; having brought about only one more human existence, he makes an end of suffering. This, Sāriputta, is the seventh individual who, passing away with a remainder, is liberated from hell… is liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.

“Puna caparaṃ, sāriputta, idhekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ mattaso kārī, paññāya mattaso kārī. So tiṇṇaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā kolaṃkolo hoti, dve vā tīṇi vā kulāni sandhāvitvā saṃsaritvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ, sāriputta, aṭṭhamo puggalo saupādiseso kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā…pe… parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

“Furthermore, Sāriputta, here a certain individual is one who fulfills virtue, but practices concentration and wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the three fetters, he becomes a ‘family-to-family’ goer; having run and wandered through two or three families, he makes an end of suffering. This, Sāriputta, is the eighth individual who, passing away with a remainder, is liberated from hell… is liberated from the planes of misery, the bad destinations, the state of ruin.

“Puna [V 3.0196] caparaṃ, sāriputta, idhekacco puggalo sīlesu paripūrakārī hoti, samādhismiṃ mattaso kārī, paññāya mattaso kārī. So tiṇṇaṃ [M 3.0187] saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā sattakkhattuparamo hoti, sattakkhattuparamaṃ deve ca manusse ca sandhāvitvā saṃsaritvā dukkhassantaṃ karoti. Ayaṃ, sāriputta, navamo puggalo saupādiseso kālaṃ kurumāno parimutto nirayā parimutto tiracchānayoniyā parimutto pettivisayā parimutto apāyaduggativinipātā.

Furthermore, Sāriputta, in this world a certain person is one who fulfills virtue, practices concentration moderately, and practices wisdom moderately. Through the destruction of the three fetters, he becomes one who is 'at most a seven-timer'; being at most a seven-timer, having run and wandered among devas and humans, he makes an end of suffering. This, Sāriputta, is the ninth person who, dying with a remainder, is liberated from hell, liberated from the animal womb, liberated from the realm of petas, and liberated from the plane of misery, the bad destination, the state of ruin.

“Ke ca, sāriputta, aññatitthiyā paribbājakā bālā abyattā, ke ca saupādisesaṃ vā ‘saupādiseso’ti jānissanti, anupādisesaṃ vā ‘anupādiseso’ti jānissanti! Ime kho, sāriputta, nava puggalā saupādisesā kālaṃ kurumānā parimuttā nirayā parimuttā tiracchānayoniyā parimuttā pettivisayā parimuttā apāyaduggativinipātā. Na tāvāyaṃ, sāriputta, dhammapariyāyo paṭibhāsi bhikkhūnaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ upāsakānaṃ upāsikānaṃ. Taṃ kissa [T 4.0396] hetu? Māyimaṃ dhammapariyāyaṃ sutvā pamādaṃ āhariṃsūti āhariṃsu (sī. pī.). Api [P 4.0382] ca mayā api cāyaṃ (?), sāriputta, dhammapariyāyo pañhādhippāyena bhāsito”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

And who, Sāriputta, are those foolish and unskilful wanderers of other sects? Who among them would know one with a remainder as 'one with a remainder,' or one without a remainder as 'one without a remainder'? Indeed, Sāriputta, these nine persons, dying with a remainder, are liberated from hell, liberated from the animal womb, liberated from the realm of petas, and liberated from the plane of misery, the bad destination, the state of ruin. This Dhamma discourse, Sāriputta, was not at first revealed to the monks, nuns, male lay followers, or female lay followers. For what reason? Lest, having heard this Dhamma discourse, they might fall into negligence. However, Sāriputta, this Dhamma discourse was spoken by me in accordance with the intention of your question. Second.

3. Koṭṭhikasuttaṃ

3. The Koṭṭhika Sutta

13. Atha kho āyasmā mahākoṭṭhiko mahākoṭṭhito (sī. syā. pī.) yenāyasmā sāriputto tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmatā sāriputtena saddhiṃ sammodi. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno kho āyasmā mahākoṭṭhiko āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ etadavoca – “kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ diṭṭhadhammavedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ samparāyavedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

13. Then the Venerable Mahākoṭṭhika approached the Venerable Sāriputta; having approached, he exchanged greetings with the Venerable Sāriputta. Having concluded a courteous and memorable conversation, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Mahākoṭṭhika said this to the Venerable Sāriputta: “Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced in the present existence become kamma to be experienced in a future life’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ sukhavedanīyaṃ sukhavedaniyaṃ (ka.) ma. ni. 3.8 passitabbaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ dukkhavedanīyaṃ dukkhavedaniyaṃ (ka.) hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced as pleasant become kamma to be experienced as painful’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ sukhavedanīyaṃ sukhavedaniyaṃ (ka.) ma. ni. 3.8 passitabbaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ dukkhavedanīyaṃ dukkhavedaniyaṃ (ka.) hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced as pleasant become kamma to be experienced as painful’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ [M 3.0188][V 3.0197] panāvuso, sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ dukkhavedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ sukhavedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced as painful become kamma to be experienced as pleasant’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ paripakkavedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ aparipakkavedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No [T 4.0397] hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced when ripened become kamma to be experienced when unripened’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ aparipakkavedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ paripakkavedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced when unripened become kamma to be experienced when ripened’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ bahuvedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ appavedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which has much to be experienced become kamma which has little to be experienced’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta[P 4.0383], ‘yaṃ kammaṃ appavedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ bahuvedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which has little to be experienced become kamma which has much to be experienced’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ vedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ avedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced become kamma which is not to be experienced’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, ‘yaṃ kammaṃ avedanīyaṃ, taṃ me kammaṃ vedanīyaṃ hotū’ti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti? “No hidaṃ, āvuso”.

“But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: ‘May that kamma of mine which is not to be experienced become kamma which is to be experienced’?” “This is not so, friend.”

“‘Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ diṭṭhadhammavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ samparāyavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced in the present existence become kamma to be experienced in a future life”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’

“‘Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ samparāyavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ diṭṭhadhammavedanīyaṃ hotūti[V 3.0198], etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced in a future life become kamma to be experienced in the present existence”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’

“‘Kiṃ [M 3.0189] nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ sukhavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ dukkhavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced as pleasant become kamma to be experienced as painful”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’

“‘Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ dukkhavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ sukhavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti [T 4.0398] puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced as painful become kamma to be experienced as pleasant”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’

“‘Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ paripakkavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ aparipakkavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced when ripened become kamma to be experienced when unripened”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’

“‘Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ aparipakkavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ paripakkavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ [P 4.0384] vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced when unripened become kamma to be experienced when ripened”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’”

“‘Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ bahuvedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ appavedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which has much to be experienced become kamma which has little to be experienced”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’”

“‘Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ appavedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ bahuvedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which has little to be experienced become kamma which has much to be experienced”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’”

“‘Kiṃ nu kho, āvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ vedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ avedanīyaṃ hotūti[V 3.0199], etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi.

“When asked, ‘Friend Sāriputta, is it that the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is to be experienced become kamma which is not to be experienced”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’”

“‘Kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, yaṃ kammaṃ avedanīyaṃ taṃ me kammaṃ vedanīyaṃ hotūti, etassa atthāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘no hidaṃ, āvuso’ti vadesi. Atha kimatthaṃ carahāvuso, bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti?

“When asked, ‘But then, friend Sāriputta, is the holy life lived under the Blessed One for the sake of this: “May that kamma of mine which is not to be experienced become kamma which is to be experienced”?’ you reply, ‘This is not so, friend.’ In that case, friend, for what purpose is the holy life lived under the Blessed One?”

“Yaṃ khvassa yaṃ kho (ka.), āvuso, aññātaṃ adiṭṭhaṃ appattaṃ asacchikataṃ anabhisametaṃ, tassa ñāṇāya dassanāya pattiyā sacchikiriyāya abhisamayāya bhagavati [T 4.0399] brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti vussati (syā.). (“Kiṃ panassāvuso, aññātaṃ [M 3.0190] adiṭṭhaṃ appattaṃ asacchikataṃ anabhisametaṃ, yassa ñāṇāya dassanāya pattiyā sacchikiriyāya abhisamayāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti?) ( ) syā. ka. potthakesu natthi “‘Idaṃ dukkha’nti khvassa kho yaṃ (ka.), āvuso, aññātaṃ adiṭṭhaṃ appattaṃ asacchikataṃ anabhisametaṃ. Tassa ñāṇāya dassanāya pattiyā sacchikiriyāya abhisamayāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussati. Ayaṃ [P 4.0385] ‘dukkhasamudayo’ti khvassa, āvuso…pe… ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodho’ti khvassa, āvuso…pe… ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti khvassa, āvuso, aññātaṃ adiṭṭhaṃ appattaṃ asacchikataṃ anabhisametaṃ. Tassa ñāṇāya dassanāya pattiyā sacchikiriyāya abhisamayāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussati. Idaṃ khvassa iti kho yaṃ (ka.), āvuso, aññātaṃ adiṭṭhaṃ appattaṃ asacchikataṃ anabhisametaṃ. Tassa yassa (?) ñāṇāya dassanāya pattiyā sacchikiriyāya abhisamayāya bhagavati brahmacariyaṃ vussatī”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“Friend, the holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the knowledge, vision, attainment, realization, and thorough understanding of that which is unknown, unseen, unattained, unrealized, and not thoroughly understood.” (“But what, friend, is unknown, unseen, unattained, unrealized, and not thoroughly understood, for the knowledge, vision, attainment, realization, and thorough understanding of which the holy life is lived under the Blessed One?”) “‘This is suffering’—this, friend, is unknown, unseen, unattained, unrealized, and not thoroughly understood. The holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the knowledge, vision, attainment, realization, and thorough understanding of this. ‘This is the origin of suffering’—this, friend…pe… ‘This is the cessation of suffering’—this, friend…pe… ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering’—this, friend, is unknown, unseen, unattained, unrealized, and not thoroughly understood. The holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the knowledge, vision, attainment, realization, and thorough understanding of this. This, friend, is unknown, unseen, unattained, unrealized, and not thoroughly understood. The holy life is lived under the Blessed One for the knowledge, vision, attainment, realization, and thorough understanding of this.” The third.

4. Samiddhisuttaṃ

4. The Discourse with Samiddhi

14. Atha kho āyasmā samiddhi yenāyasmā sāriputto tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmantaṃ sāriputtaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho āyasmantaṃ samiddhiṃ āyasmā sāriputto etadavoca – “kimārammaṇā, samiddhi, purisassa saṅkappavitakkā uppajjantī”ti? “Nāmarūpārammaṇā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kva nānattaṃ gacchantī”ti? “Dhātūsu, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsamudayā”ti? “Phassasamudayā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsamosaraṇā”ti? “Vedanāsamosaraṇā, bhante”ti. “Te pana[V 3.0200], samiddhi, kiṃpamukhā”ti? “Samādhippamukhā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃadhipateyyā”ti? “Satādhipateyyā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃuttarā”ti? “Paññuttarā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsārā”ti? “Vimuttisārā, bhante”ti. “Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃogadhā”ti? “Amatogadhā, bhante”ti.

14. Then the Venerable Samiddhi went to the Venerable Sāriputta; having gone, he paid homage to the Venerable Sāriputta and sat down to one side. To the Venerable Samiddhi sitting to one side, the Venerable Sāriputta said this: “Samiddhi, with what as their object do a person’s thoughts of intention arise?” “Venerable sir, with name-and-form as their object.” “But, Samiddhi, wherein do they become diverse?” “Venerable sir, in the elements.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their origin?” “Venerable sir, contact is their origin.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their meeting place?” “Venerable sir, feeling is their meeting place.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their chief?” “Venerable sir, concentration is their chief.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their governing principle?” “Venerable sir, mindfulness is their governing principle.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their superior?” “Venerable sir, wisdom is their superior.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their essence?” “Venerable sir, liberation is their essence.” “But, Samiddhi, what is their foundation?” “Venerable sir, the Deathless is their foundation.”

“‘Kimārammaṇā, samiddhi, purisassa saṅkappavitakkā uppajjantī’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘nāmarūpārammaṇā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kva [T 4.0400] nānattaṃ gacchantī’ti, iti [P 4.0386] puṭṭho samāno ‘dhātūsu, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsamudayā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘phassasamudayā, bhante’ti [M 3.0191] vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsamosaraṇā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘vedanāsamosaraṇā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃpamukhā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘samādhippamukhā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃadhipateyyā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘satādhipateyyā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃuttarā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘paññuttarā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃsārā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘vimuttisārā, bhante’ti vadesi. ‘Te pana, samiddhi, kiṃogadhā’ti, iti puṭṭho samāno ‘amatogadhā, bhante’ti vadesi. Sādhu sādhu, samiddhi! Sādhu kho tvaṃ, samiddhi, puṭṭho pañhaṃ (sī. syā. pī.) puṭṭho vissajjesi, tena ca mā maññī”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“When asked, ‘Samiddhi, with what as their object do a person’s thoughts of intention arise?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, with name-and-form as their object.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, wherein do they become diverse?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, in the elements.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their origin?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, contact is their origin.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their meeting place?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, feeling is their meeting place.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their chief?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, concentration is their chief.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their governing principle?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, mindfulness is their governing principle.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their superior?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, wisdom is their superior.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their essence?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, liberation is their essence.’ When asked, ‘But, Samiddhi, what is their foundation?’ you replied, ‘Venerable sir, the Deathless is their foundation.’ Good, good, Samiddhi! It is good, Samiddhi, that being asked again and again, you have answered; but do not be conceited on account of that.” The fourth.

5. Gaṇḍasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Boil

15. “Seyyathāpi, bhikkhave, gaṇḍo anekavassagaṇiko. Tassassu gaṇḍassa nava vaṇamukhāni nava abhedanamukhāni. Tato yaṃ kiñci pagghareyya – asuciyeva pagghareyya, duggandhaṃyeva pagghareyya, jegucchiyaṃyeva jegucchiyeva (ka.) pagghareyya; yaṃ kiñci pasaveyya – asuciyeva pasaveyya, duggandhaṃyeva pasaveyya, jegucchiyaṃyeva pasaveyya.

15. “Bhikkhus, suppose there were a boil that had lasted for many years. And that boil had nine wound-openings, nine unburst openings. Whatever would seep from it—only what is impure would seep, only what is foul-smelling would seep, only what is disgusting would seep; whatever it would produce—it would produce only what is impure, it would produce only what is foul-smelling, it would produce only what is disgusting.

“Gaṇḍoti kho, bhikkhave, imassetaṃ cātumahābhūtikassa cātummahābhūtikassa (sī. syā. pī.) kāyassa adhivacanaṃ mātāpettikasambhavassa odanakummāsūpacayassa aniccucchādanaparimaddanabhedanaviddhaṃsanadhammassa. Tassassu gaṇḍassa nava vaṇamukhāni nava abhedanamukhāni. Tato yaṃ kiñci paggharati – asuciyeva paggharati, duggandhaṃyeva paggharati, jegucchiyaṃyeva [P 4.0387] paggharati; yaṃ kiñci pasavati [T 4.0401] – asuciyeva [V 3.0201] pasavati, duggandhaṃyeva pasavati, jegucchiyaṃyeva pasavati. Tasmātiha, bhikkhave, imasmiṃ kāye nibbindathā”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

“‘Boil,’ bhikkhus, is a designation for this body composed of the four great elements, born of mother and father, built up of rice and porridge, and subject to impermanence, to being rubbed and massaged, to breaking apart and dispersal. This boil has nine wound-openings, nine unburst openings. Whatever seeps from it, only what is impure seeps, only what is foul-smelling seeps, only what is disgusting seeps. Whatever it produces, it produces only what is impure, it produces only what is foul-smelling, it produces only what is disgusting. Therefore, bhikkhus, become disenchanted with this body.” Fifth.

6. Saññāsuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Perceptions

16. “Navayimā, bhikkhave, saññā bhāvitā bahulīkatā mahapphalā honti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā. Katamā nava[M 3.0192]? Asubhasaññā, maraṇasaññā, āhāre paṭikūlasaññā paṭikkūlasaññā (sī. syā. pī.), sabbaloke anabhiratasaññā anabhiratisaññā (ka.) a. ni. 5.121-122, aniccasaññā, anicce dukkhasaññā, dukkhe anattasaññā, pahānasaññā, virāgasaññā – imā kho, bhikkhave, nava saññā, bhāvitā bahulīkatā mahapphalā honti mahānisaṃsā amatogadhā amatapariyosānā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

16. “Bhikkhus, these nine perceptions, when developed and cultivated, are of great fruit and great benefit, have the Deathless as their ground, and the Deathless as their culmination. Which nine? The perception of the foul, the perception of death, the perception of loathsomeness in food, the perception of non-delight in the entire world, the perception of impermanence, the perception of suffering in the impermanent, the perception of not-self in what is suffering, the perception of abandoning, and the perception of dispassion. These, bhikkhus, are the nine perceptions that, when developed and cultivated, are of great fruit and great benefit, have the Deathless as their ground, and the Deathless as their culmination.” Sixth.

7. Kulasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Families

17. “Navahi, bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgataṃ kulaṃ anupagantvā vā nālaṃ upagantuṃ, upagantvā vā nālaṃ nisīdituṃ. Katamehi navahi? Na manāpena paccuṭṭhenti, na manāpena abhivādenti, na manāpena āsanaṃ denti, santamassa pariguhanti, bahukampi thokaṃ denti, paṇītampi lūkhaṃ denti, asakkaccaṃ denti no sakkaccaṃ, na upanisīdanti dhammassavanāya, bhāsitamassa na sussūsanti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, navahaṅgehi samannāgataṃ kulaṃ anupagantvā vā nālaṃ upagantuṃ upagantvā vā nālaṃ nisīdituṃ.

17. “Bhikkhus, a family endowed with nine factors is not fit to approach if one has not yet approached, nor is it fit to sit with if one has approached. With which nine? They do not rise up pleasantly, they do not pay homage pleasantly, they do not offer a seat pleasantly; they conceal what they have from him; though they have much, they give little; though they have fine things, they give coarse things; they give disrespectfully, not respectfully; they do not sit near to hear the Dhamma; they do not listen well to what he has spoken. A family endowed with these nine factors, bhikkhus, is not fit to approach if one has not yet approached, nor is it fit to sit with if one has approached.

“Navahi, bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgataṃ kulaṃ anupagantvā vā alaṃ upagantuṃ, upagantvā vā alaṃ nisīdituṃ. Katamehi navahi? Manāpena paccuṭṭhenti, manāpena abhivādenti, manāpena āsanaṃ denti, santamassa [T 4.0402] na pariguhanti, bahukampi [P 4.0388] bahukaṃ denti, paṇītampi paṇītaṃ denti, sakkaccaṃ denti no asakkaccaṃ, upanisīdanti dhammassavanāya, bhāsitamassa sussūsanti. Imehi kho, bhikkhave, navahaṅgehi samannāgataṃ kulaṃ anupagantvā vā alaṃ upagantuṃ, upagantvā vā alaṃ nisīditu”nti. Sattamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, a family endowed with nine factors is fit to approach if one has not yet approached, and it is fit to sit with if one has approached. With which nine? They rise up pleasantly, they pay homage pleasantly, they offer a seat pleasantly; they do not conceal what they have from him; though they have much, they give much; though they have fine things, they give fine things; they give respectfully, not disrespectfully; they sit near to hear the Dhamma; they listen well to what he has spoken. A family endowed with these nine factors, bhikkhus, is fit to approach if one has not yet approached, and it is fit to sit with if one has approached.” Seventh.

8. Navaṅguposathasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on the Uposatha with Nine Factors

18. “Navahi[V 3.0202], bhikkhave, aṅgehi samannāgato uposatho upavuttho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro. Kathaṃ upavuttho ca, bhikkhave, navahaṅgehi samannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro? Idha, bhikkhave, ariyasāvako iti paṭisañcikkhati – ‘yāvajīvaṃ arahanto pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭiviratā nihitadaṇḍā nihitasatthā lajjī [M 3.0193] dayāpannā sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampino viharanti; ahampajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ pāṇātipātaṃ pahāya pāṇātipātā paṭivirato nihitadaṇḍo nihitasattho lajjī dayāpanno sabbapāṇabhūtahitānukampī viharāmi. Imināpaṅgena imināpi aṅgena (ka. sī.) arahataṃ anukaromi; uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā paṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti…pe. [P 4.0389]….

18. “Bhikkhus, the Uposatha, when observed endowed with nine factors, is of great fruit, great benefit, great splendor, and great pervasiveness. And how, bhikkhus, is the Uposatha, when observed endowed with nine factors, of great fruit, great benefit, great splendor, and great pervasiveness? Here, bhikkhus, a noble disciple reflects thus: ‘For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned the destruction of life, abstain from the destruction of life; they dwell with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, conscientious, merciful, and compassionate for the welfare of all living beings. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned the destruction of life, will abstain from the destruction of life; I will dwell with rod laid down, with weapon laid down, conscientious, merciful, and compassionate for the welfare of all living beings. By this factor I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this first factor… and so on.

“‘Yāvajīvaṃ arahanto uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭiviratā nīcaseyyaṃ kappenti [P 4.0390] – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā; ahampajja imañca rattiṃ imañca divasaṃ uccāsayanamahāsayanaṃ pahāya uccāsayanamahāsayanā paṭivirato nīcaseyyaṃ kappemi – mañcake vā tiṇasanthārake vā. Imināpaṅgena arahataṃ [T 4.0403] anukaromi; uposatho ca me upavuttho bhavissatī’ti. Iminā aṭṭhamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti.

“‘For as long as they live, the Arahants, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, abstain from high and luxurious beds; they make use of a low resting place, on a couch or a straw mat. Today, for this night and this day, I too, having abandoned high and luxurious beds, will abstain from high and luxurious beds; I will make use of a low resting place, on a couch or a straw mat. By this factor I emulate the Arahants, and my Uposatha will have been observed.’ He is endowed with this eighth factor.

“Mettāsahagatena cetasā ekaṃ disaṃ pharitvā viharati, tathā dutiyaṃ tathā tatiyaṃ tathā catutthaṃ. Iti uddhamadho tiriyaṃ sabbadhi sabbattatāya sabbāvantaṃ lokaṃ mettāsahagatena cetasā vipulena mahaggatena appamāṇena averena abyāpajjena abyāpajjhena (ka.), abyābajjhena (?) pharitvā viharati. Iminā navamena aṅgena samannāgato hoti. Evaṃ upavuttho kho, bhikkhave, navahaṅgehi samannāgato uposatho mahapphalo hoti mahānisaṃso mahājutiko mahāvipphāro”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“With a mind accompanied by loving-kindness, he dwells pervading one direction, likewise the second, likewise the third, likewise the fourth. Thus above, below, and across; everywhere, and to all as to himself, he dwells pervading the entire world with a mind accompanied by loving-kindness—abundant, exalted, immeasurable, without enmity, and without ill will. He is endowed with this ninth factor. Thus, bhikkhus, the Uposatha, when observed endowed with these nine factors, is of great fruit, great benefit, great splendor, and great pervasiveness.” Eighth.

9. Devatāsuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Deities

19. “Imañca, bhikkhave, rattiṃ sambahulā devatā abhikkantāya rattiyā abhikkantavaṇṇā kevalakappaṃ jetavanaṃ obhāsetvā yenāhaṃ tenupasaṅkamiṃsu; upasaṅkamitvā maṃ abhivādetvā [V 3.0203] ekamantaṃ aṭṭhaṃsu. Ekamantaṃ ṭhitā kho, bhikkhave, tā devatā maṃ etadavocuṃ – ‘upasaṅkamiṃsu no, bhante, pubbe manussabhūtānaṃ pabbajitā agārāni. Te mayaṃ, bhante, paccuṭṭhimha, no ca kho abhivādimha. Tā mayaṃ, bhante, aparipuṇṇakammantā vippaṭisāriniyo paccānutāpiniyo hīnaṃ kāyaṃ upapannā’”ti.

19. “Bhikkhus, just this night, many deities, when the night was far advanced, of surpassing beauty, illuminating the entire Jeta’s Grove, approached me. Having approached and paid homage to me, they stood to one side. Standing to one side, bhikkhus, those deities said this to me: ‘Venerable Sir, formerly, when we were human beings, the ordained approached our homes. We, Venerable Sir, rose to greet them, but did not pay homage. We, Venerable Sir, because of that incomplete deed, being remorseful and regretful, have been reborn in an inferior celestial body.’”

“Aparāpi [M 3.0194][P 4.0391] maṃ, bhikkhave, sambahulā devatā upasaṅkamitvā etadavocuṃ – ‘upasaṅkamiṃsu no, bhante, pubbe manussabhūtānaṃ pabbajitā agārāni. Te mayaṃ, bhante, paccuṭṭhimha abhivādimha paccuṭṭhimha ca abhivādimha ca (syā.), no ca tesaṃ āsanaṃ adamha. Tā mayaṃ, bhante, aparipuṇṇakammantā vippaṭisāriniyo paccānutāpiniyo hīnaṃ kāyaṃ upapannā’”ti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, another group of many deities approached me and said this: ‘Venerable Sir, formerly, when we were human beings, the ordained approached our homes. We, Venerable Sir, rose to greet them and paid homage, but we did not offer them a seat. We, Venerable Sir, because of that incomplete deed, being remorseful and regretful, have been reborn in an inferior celestial body.’”

“Aparāpi maṃ, bhikkhave, sambahulā devatā upasaṅkamitvā [T 4.0404] etadavocuṃ – ‘upasaṅkamiṃsu no, bhante, pubbe manussabhūtānaṃ pabbajitā agārāni. Te mayaṃ, bhante, paccuṭṭhimha abhivādimha paccuṭṭhimha ca abhivādimha ca (syā.) āsanaṃ āsanañca (sī. syā.) adamha, no ca kho yathāsatti yathābalaṃ saṃvibhajimha…pe… yathāsatti yathābalaṃ yathābalaṃ ca (?) saṃvibhajimha, no ca kho upanisīdimha dhammassavanāya…pe… upanisīdimha upanisīdimha ca (syā.) dhammassavanāya, no ca kho ohitasotā dhammaṃ suṇimha…pe… ohitasotā ca dhammaṃ suṇimha, no ca kho sutvā dhammaṃ dhārayimha…pe… sutvā ca dhammaṃ dhārayimha, no ca kho dhātānaṃ dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhimha…pe… dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhimha, no ca kho atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammaṃ paṭipajjimha. Tā mayaṃ, bhante, aparipuṇṇakammantā vippaṭisāriniyo paccānutāpiniyo hīnaṃ kāyaṃ upapannā’”ti.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, another group of many deities approached me and said this: ‘Venerable Sir, formerly, when we were human beings, the ordained approached our homes. We, Venerable Sir, rose to greet them, paid homage, and offered a seat, but we did not share according to our ability and strength… We shared according to our ability and strength, but we did not sit near to hear the Dhamma… We sat near to hear the Dhamma, but we did not listen to the Dhamma with attentive ears… We listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears, but having heard the Dhamma, we did not bear it in mind… Having heard and borne the Dhamma in mind, we did not investigate the meaning of those teachings that were borne in mind… Having investigated the meaning of those teachings, and having understood the meaning and the teaching, we did not practice in accordance with the Dhamma. We, Venerable Sir, because of that incomplete deed, being remorseful and regretful, have been reborn in an inferior celestial body.’”

“Aparāpi maṃ, bhikkhave, sambahulā devatā upasaṅkamitvā etadavocuṃ – ‘upasaṅkamiṃsu no, bhante, pubbe manussabhūtānaṃ pabbajitā agārāni. Te mayaṃ, bhante, paccuṭṭhimha abhivādimha paccuṭṭhimha ca abhivādimha ca (syā.), āsanaṃ āsanañca (sī. syā.) adamha, yathāsatti [P 4.0392] yathābalaṃ yathābalaṃ ca (?) saṃvibhajimha, upanisīdimha upanisīdimha ca (syā.) dhammassavanāya, ohitasotā ca dhammaṃ suṇimha, sutvā ca dhammaṃ dhārayimha, dhātānañca dhammānaṃ atthaṃ upaparikkhimha, atthamaññāya dhammamaññāya dhammānudhammaṃ dhammānudhammañca (?) paṭipajjimha. Tā mayaṃ, bhante, paripuṇṇakammantā avippaṭisāriniyo apaccānutāpiniyo paṇītaṃ kāyaṃ upapannā’ti. Etāni, bhikkhave, rukkhamūlāni etāni suññāgārāni. Jhāyatha, bhikkhave, mā pamādattha, mā pacchā vippaṭisārino ahuvattha seyyathāpi tā purimikā devatā”ti. Navamaṃ.

“Furthermore, bhikkhus, another group of many deities approached me and said this: ‘Venerable Sir, formerly, when we were human beings, the ordained approached our homes. We, Venerable Sir, rose to greet them, paid homage, offered a seat, shared according to our ability and strength, sat near to hear the Dhamma, listened to the Dhamma with attentive ears, having heard the Dhamma we bore it in mind, we investigated the meaning of those teachings that were borne in mind, and having understood the meaning and the teaching, we practiced in accordance with the Dhamma. We, Venerable Sir, because of that complete deed, being without remorse and without regret, have been reborn in a sublime celestial body.’ These, bhikkhus, are the roots of trees; these are secluded dwellings. Meditate, bhikkhus! Do not be heedless, lest you later become remorseful like those former deities.” Ninth.

10. Velāmasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse to Velāma

20. Ekaṃ [T 4.0405][M 3.0195][V 3.0204] samayaṃ bhagavā sāvatthiyaṃ viharati jetavane anāthapiṇḍikassa ārāme. Atha kho anāthapiṇḍiko gahapati yena bhagavā tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā bhagavantaṃ abhivādetvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinnaṃ kho anāthapiṇḍikaṃ gahapatiṃ bhagavā etadavoca –

20. On one occasion the Blessed One was dwelling at Sāvatthī in Jeta’s Grove, Anāthapiṇḍika’s Park. Then the householder Anāthapiṇḍika approached the Blessed One, paid homage to him, and sat down to one side. The Blessed One then said to the householder Anāthapiṇḍika, who was sitting to one side:

“Api nu te, gahapati, kule dānaṃ dīyatī”ti? “Dīyati me, bhante, kule dānaṃ; tañca kho lūkhaṃ kaṇājakaṃ biḷaṅgadutiya”nti. “Lūkhañcepi lūkhaṃ vāpi (syā.), lūkhañcāpi (ka.), gahapati, dānaṃ deti paṇītaṃ vā; tañca asakkaccaṃ deti, acittīkatvā acittiṃ katvā (ka.), apacittiṃ katvā (syā.), acittikatvā (pī.) deti, asahatthā deti, apaviddhaṃ apaviṭṭhaṃ (syā.) deti, anāgamanadiṭṭhiko deti. Yattha yattha tassa tassa dānassa vipāko nibbattati, na uḷārāya bhattabhogāya cittaṃ namati, na uḷārāya vatthabhogāya cittaṃ namati, na uḷārāya yānabhogāya cittaṃ namati, na uḷāresu pañcasu kāmaguṇesu bhogāya cittaṃ namati. Yepissa te honti [P 4.0393] puttāti vā dārāti vā dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā, tepi na sussūsanti na sotaṃ odahanti na aññā cittaṃ upaṭṭhapenti. Taṃ kissa hetu? Evañhetaṃ evañcetaṃ (syā. ka.), gahapati, hoti asakkaccaṃ katānaṃ kammānaṃ vipāko”.

“Householder, is giving practiced in your family?” “Venerable sir, giving is practiced in my family, but it is coarse: broken rice with sour gruel as the second dish.” “Householder, whether one gives a coarse or a fine gift, if one gives it disrespectfully, without consideration, not with one’s own hand, as something discarded, without a view of its future result, then wherever the result of that gift ripens, one’s mind does not incline toward the enjoyment of fine food, fine clothing, or fine vehicles; it does not incline toward the enjoyment of the five fine kinds of sensual pleasure. And one’s children, wife, slaves, messengers, and workers do not wish to listen, they do not lend an ear, and they do not apply their minds to understand. For what reason? Because, householder, such is the result of deeds done disrespectfully.”

“Lūkhañcepi, gahapati, dānaṃ deti paṇītaṃ vā; tañca sakkaccaṃ deti, cittīkatvā deti, sahatthā deti, anapaviddhaṃ deti, āgamanadiṭṭhiko deti. Yattha yattha tassa tassa dānassa vipāko nibbattati, uḷārāya bhattabhogāya cittaṃ namati, uḷārāya vatthabhogāya cittaṃ namati, uḷārāya yānabhogāya cittaṃ namati, uḷāresu pañcasu kāmaguṇesu bhogāya cittaṃ namati. Yepissa te honti puttāti [T 4.0406] vā dārāti vā dāsāti vā pessāti vā kammakarāti vā, tepi sussūsanti sotaṃ odahanti aññā cittaṃ upaṭṭhapenti. Taṃ kissa hetu? Evañhetaṃ, gahapati, hoti sakkaccaṃ katānaṃ kammānaṃ vipāko.

“Householder, whether one gives a coarse or a fine gift, if one gives it respectfully, with consideration, with one’s own hand, not as something discarded, and with a view of its future result, then wherever the result of that gift ripens, one’s mind inclines toward the enjoyment of fine food, fine clothing, and fine vehicles; it inclines toward the enjoyment of the five fine kinds of sensual pleasure. And one’s children, wife, slaves, messengers, and workers wish to listen, they lend an ear, and they apply their minds to understand. For what reason? Because, householder, such is the result of deeds done respectfully.”

“Bhūtapubbaṃ, gahapati, velāmo nāma brāhmaṇo ahosi. So evarūpaṃ dānaṃ adāsi mahādānaṃ. Caturāsīti suvaṇṇapātisahassāni adāsi rūpiyapūrāni[M 3.0196], caturāsīti rūpiyapātisahassāni adāsi suvaṇṇapūrāni, caturāsīti kaṃsapātisahassāni adāsi hiraññapūrāni[V 3.0205], caturāsīti hatthisahassāni adāsi sovaṇṇālaṅkārāni sovaṇṇadhajāni hemajālappaṭicchannāni hemajālasañchannāni (sī. pī.), caturāsīti rathasahassāni adāsi sīhacammaparivārāni byagghacammaparivārāni dīpicammaparivārāni paṇḍukambalaparivārāni sovaṇṇālaṅkārāni sovaṇṇadhajāni hemajālappaṭicchannāni, caturāsīti dhenusahassāni adāsi dukūlasandhanāni dukūlasandassanāni (sī.), dukūlasaṇṭhanāni (syā.), dukūlasanthanāni (pī.), duhasandanāni (dī. ni. 2.263), dukūlasandanāni (tattha pāṭhantaraṃ) kaṃsūpadhāraṇāni, caturāsīti kaññāsahassāni adāsi āmuttamaṇikuṇḍalāyo āmukkamaṇikuṇḍalāyo (?), caturāsīti pallaṅkasahassāni adāsi gonakatthatāni [P 4.0394] paṭikatthatāni paṭalikatthatāni kadalimigapavarapaccattharaṇāni sauttaracchadāni ubhatolohitakūpadhānāni, caturāsīti vatthakoṭisahassāni adāsi khomasukhumānaṃ koseyyasukhumānaṃ kambalasukhumānaṃ kappāsikasukhumānaṃ, ko pana vādo annassa pānassa khajjassa bhojjassa leyyassa peyyassa, najjo maññe vissandanti vissandati (sī. pī.).

“Once in the past, householder, there was a brahmin named Velāma. He gave such a great gift, a great alms-giving. He gave eighty-four thousand golden bowls filled with silver; eighty-four thousand silver bowls filled with gold; eighty-four thousand bronze bowls filled with bullion; eighty-four thousand elephants with golden ornaments and golden banners, covered with nets of gold; eighty-four thousand chariots upholstered with lion skins, tiger skins, and leopard skins, with yellow blankets, with golden ornaments and golden banners, covered with nets of gold; eighty-four thousand milch cows with fine linen tethers and bronze milk pails; eighty-four thousand maidens adorned with jeweled earrings; eighty-four thousand couches spread with long-fleeced coverlets, white woolen coverlets, and embroidered coverlets, with spreads of the finest antelope hides, with canopies above and red cushions at both ends; eighty-four thousand crores of garments of fine linen, fine silk, fine wool, and fine cotton. And what can be said of the food and drink, the edibles and comestibles, the syrups and beverages? They flowed, it seemed, like rivers.”

“Siyā kho pana te, gahapati, evamassa – ‘añño nūna tena samayena velāmo brāhmaṇo ahosi, so yo (?) taṃ dānaṃ adāsi mahādāna’nti. Na kho panetaṃ, gahapati, evaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Ahaṃ tena samayena velāmo brāhmaṇo [T 4.0407] ahosiṃ. Ahaṃ taṃ dānaṃ adāsiṃ mahādānaṃ. Tasmiṃ kho pana, gahapati, dāne na koci dakkhiṇeyyo ahosi, na taṃ koci dakkhiṇaṃ visodheti.

“Now, householder, it might occur to you: ‘Perhaps the brahmin Velāma who on that occasion gave that great gift was someone else.’ But it should not be seen like this. I myself was the brahmin Velāma on that occasion. I gave that great gift, that great alms-giving. But in that gift, householder, there was no one worthy of offerings; no one purified that offering.”

“Yaṃ, gahapati, velāmo brāhmaṇo dānaṃ adāsi mahādānaṃ, yo cekaṃ diṭṭhisampannaṃ bhojeyya, idaṃ tato mahapphalataraṃ.

“Householder, more fruitful than the great gift that the brahmin Velāma gave would be to feed one person accomplished in view.”

( ) (yañca gahapati velāmo brāhmaṇo dānaṃ adāsi mahādānaṃ) (sī. pī.) “Yo ca sataṃ diṭṭhisampannānaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ sakadāgāmiṃ bhojeyya, idaṃ tato mahapphalataraṃ.

“More fruitful than feeding a hundred persons accomplished in view would be to feed one once-returner.”

( ) (yañca gahapati velāmo brāhmaṇo dānaṃ adāsi mahādānaṃ) (sī. pī.) “Yo ca sataṃ sakadāgāmīnaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ anāgāmiṃ bhojeyya…pe… yo ca sataṃ anāgāmīnaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ [M 3.0197] arahantaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ arahantānaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ paccekabuddhaṃ bhojeyya [P 4.0395]… yo ca sataṃ paccekabuddhānaṃ bhojeyya, yo ca tathāgataṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca cātuddisaṃ saṅghaṃ uddissa vihāraṃ kārāpeyya… yo ca pasannacitto buddhañca dhammañca saṅghañca saraṇaṃ gaccheyya… yo ca pasannacitto sikkhāpadāni samādiyeyya – pāṇātipātā veramaṇiṃ, adinnādānā [V 3.0206] veramaṇiṃ, kāmesumicchācārā veramaṇiṃ, musāvādā veramaṇiṃ, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā veramaṇiṃ, yo ca antamaso gandhohanamattampi gandhūhanamattampi (sī.), gaddūhanamattampi (syā. pī.) ma. ni. 3.211 mettacittaṃ bhāveyya, ( ) (yo ca accharāsaṅghātamattampi aniccasaññaṃ bhāveyya) (ka.) idaṃ tato mahapphalataraṃ.

“More fruitful than feeding a hundred once-returners would be to feed one non-returner; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred non-returners would be to feed one arahant; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred arahants would be to feed one paccekabuddha; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred paccekabuddhas would be to feed the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One; … more fruitful than feeding the Tathāgata would be to feed the Sangha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha; … more fruitful than feeding the Sangha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha would be to have a monastery built dedicated to the Sangha of the four quarters; … more fruitful than that would be to go for refuge with a confident mind to the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Sangha; … more fruitful than that would be to undertake with a confident mind the training rules—abstention from killing living beings, from taking what is not given, from sexual misconduct, from false speech, and from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness; … more fruitful than that would be to develop a mind of loving-kindness, even for as long as it takes to inhale a fragrance. This would be more fruitful than that.”

“Yañca, gahapati, velāmo brāhmaṇo dānaṃ adāsi mahādānaṃ, yo cekaṃ diṭṭhisampannaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ diṭṭhisampannānaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ sakadāgāmiṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ sakadāgāmīnaṃ bhojeyya, yo [T 4.0408] cekaṃ anāgāmiṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ anāgāmīnaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ arahantaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ arahantānaṃ bhojeyya, yo cekaṃ paccekabuddhaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca sataṃ paccekabuddhānaṃ bhojeyya, yo ca tathāgataṃ arahantaṃ sammāsambuddhaṃ bhojeyya… yo ca buddhappamukhaṃ bhikkhusaṅghaṃ bhojeyya, yo ca cātuddisaṃ saṅghaṃ uddissa vihāraṃ kārāpeyya… yo ca pasannacitto buddhañca dhammañca saṅghañca saraṇaṃ gaccheyya, yo ca pasannacitto sikkhāpadāni samādiyeyya – pāṇātipātā veramaṇiṃ… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānā veramaṇiṃ, yo ca antamaso gandhohanamattampi mettacittaṃ bhāveyya[P 4.0396], yo ca accharāsaṅghātamattampi aniccasaññaṃ bhāveyya, idaṃ tato mahapphalatara”nti. Dasamaṃ.

“Householder, more fruitful than the great gift that the brahmin Velāma gave is to feed one person accomplished in view; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred persons accomplished in view is to feed one once-returner; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred once-returners is to feed one non-returner; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred non-returners is to feed one Arahant; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred Arahants is to feed one Paccekabuddha; … more fruitful than feeding a hundred Paccekabuddhas is to feed the Tathāgata, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One; … more fruitful than feeding the Tathāgata is to feed the Sangha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha; … more fruitful than feeding the Sangha of bhikkhus headed by the Buddha is to have a monastery built dedicated to the Sangha of the four quarters; … more fruitful than having a monastery built dedicated to the Sangha of the four quarters is to go for refuge with a confident mind to the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Sangha; … more fruitful than going for refuge with a confident mind is to undertake with a confident mind the training rules—abstinence from killing living beings… abstinence from distilled and fermented intoxicants which are the basis for heedlessness; … more fruitful than undertaking the training rules is to develop a mind of loving-kindness, even for as long as it takes to inhale a fragrance; and should one develop the perception of impermanence, even for the duration of a finger-snap, this would be of much greater fruit than that.”

Sīhanādavaggo dutiyo.

The Second Chapter on the Lion’s Roar.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary:

Nādo saupādiseso ca, koṭṭhikena samiddhinā;

Gaṇḍasaññā kulaṃ mettā, devatā velāmena cāti.

Nāda, Saupādisesa, Koṭṭhika, Samiddhi, Saññā, Kula, Mettā, Devatā, and Velāma.

3. Sattāvāsavaggo

3. The Chapter on the Abodes of Beings

1. Tiṭhānasuttaṃ

1. The Three Grounds

21. “Tīhi[M 3.0198][V 3.0207], bhikkhave, ṭhānehi uttarakurukā manussā deve ca tāvatiṃse adhiggaṇhanti jambudīpake ca manusse. Katamehi tīhi? Amamā, apariggahā, niyatāyukā, visesaguṇā visesabhuno (sī. syā. pī.) – imehi kho, bhikkhave, tīhi ṭhānehi uttarakurukā [T 4.0409] manussā deve ca tāvatiṃse adhiggaṇhanti jambudīpake ca manusse.

21. “Bhikkhus, on three grounds the humans of Uttarakuru surpass the Tāvatiṃsa devas and the humans of Jambudīpa. What three? They are without possessiveness and acquisitions, they have a fixed lifespan, and they possess special qualities. It is on these three grounds, bhikkhus, that the humans of Uttarakuru surpass the Tāvatiṃsa devas and the humans of Jambudīpa.”

“Tīhi, bhikkhave, ṭhānehi devā tāvatiṃsā uttarakuruke ca manusse adhiggaṇhanti jambudīpake ca manusse. Katamehi tīhi? Dibbena āyunā, dibbena vaṇṇena, dibbena sukhena – imehi kho, bhikkhave, tīhi ṭhānehi devā tāvatiṃsā uttarakuruke ca manusse adhiggaṇhanti jambudīpake ca manusse.

“Bhikkhus, on three grounds the Tāvatiṃsa devas surpass the humans of Uttarakuru and the humans of Jambudīpa. What three? By means of their divine lifespan, divine beauty, and divine happiness. It is on these three grounds, bhikkhus, that the Tāvatiṃsa devas surpass the humans of Uttarakuru and the humans of Jambudīpa.”

kathā. 271 “Tīhi, bhikkhave, ṭhānehi jambudīpakā manussā uttarakuruke ca manusse adhiggaṇhanti deve ca tāvatiṃse. Katamehi tīhi? Sūrā, satimanto, idha brahmacariyavāso – imehi kho, bhikkhave, tīhi ṭhānehi jambudīpakā manussā uttarakuruke ca manusse adhiggaṇhanti deve ca tāvatiṃse”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

Bhikkhus, on three grounds the humans of Jambudīpa surpass the humans of Uttarakuru and the Tāvatiṃsa devas. What three? They are courageous, mindful, and here the holy life is lived. It is on these three grounds, bhikkhus, that the humans of Jambudīpa surpass the humans of Uttarakuru and the Tāvatiṃsa devas. First.

2. Assakhaḷuṅkasuttaṃ

2. The Sutta on the Wretched Horses

22. a. ni. 3.141 “Tayo [P 4.0397] ca, bhikkhave, assakhaḷuṅke desessāmi tayo ca purisakhaḷuṅke tayo ca assaparasse assasadasse (sī. syā. pī.) a. ni. 3.142 tayo ca purisaparasse purisasadasse (sī. syā. pī.) tayo ca bhadde assājānīye tayo ca bhadde purisājānīye. Taṃ suṇātha.

22. Bhikkhus, I shall teach of the three wretched horses and the three wretched persons; the three superior horses and the three superior persons; and the three excellent thoroughbred horses and the three excellent thoroughbred persons. Listen to that.

“Katame ca, bhikkhave, tayo assakhaḷuṅkā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco assakhaḷuṅko javasampanno [V 3.0208] hoti, na vaṇṇasampanno, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno. Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco assakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno. Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco assakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime kho, bhikkhave, tayo assakhaḷuṅkā.

And what, bhikkhus, are the three wretched horses? Here, bhikkhus, a certain wretched horse is endowed with speed, but not with beauty, nor with height and girth. Then, bhikkhus, a certain wretched horse is endowed with speed and with beauty, but not with height and girth. Then, bhikkhus, a certain wretched horse is endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth. These, bhikkhus, are the three wretched horses.

“Katame [T 4.0410][M 3.0199] ca, bhikkhave, tayo purisakhaḷuṅkā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno hoti, na vaṇṇasampanno, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno. Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno. Idha pana, bhikkhave, ekacco purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca.

And what, bhikkhus, are the three wretched persons? Here, bhikkhus, a certain wretched person is endowed with speed, but not with beauty, nor with height and girth. Then, bhikkhus, a certain wretched person is endowed with speed and with beauty, but not with height and girth. Then, bhikkhus, a certain wretched person is endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth.

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno hoti, na vaṇṇasampanno na ārohapariṇāhasampanno? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu ‘idaṃ dukkha’nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, ‘ayaṃ dukkhasamudayo’ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodho’ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti, ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Idamassa [P 4.0398] javasmiṃ vadāmi. Abhidhamme kho pana abhivinaye pañhaṃ puṭṭho saṃsādeti saṃsāreti (ka.) a. ni. 1.3.141, no vissajjeti. Idamassa na vaṇṇasmiṃ vadāmi. Na kho pana lābhī hoti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Idamassa na ārohapariṇāhasmiṃ vadāmi. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno hoti, na vaṇṇasampanno na ārohapariṇāhasampanno.

And how, bhikkhus, is a wretched person endowed with speed, but not with beauty nor with height and girth? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu understands as it truly is: ‘This is suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the origin of suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the cessation of suffering’; he understands as it truly is: ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ This, I say, is his speed. But when asked a question on the Abhidhamma and the Abhivinaya, he falters and cannot answer. This, I say, is not his beauty. And he is not a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicines and provisions for the sick. This, I say, is not his height and girth. It is in this way, bhikkhus, that a wretched person is endowed with speed, but not with beauty nor with height and girth.

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu ‘idaṃ dukkha’nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Idamassa javasmiṃ vadāmi. Abhidhamme kho pana abhivinaye pañhaṃ [T 4.0411] puṭṭho vissajjeti, no saṃsādeti. Idamassa vaṇṇasmiṃ vadāmi. Na kho pana lābhī hoti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Idamassa na ārohapariṇāhasmiṃ vadāmi. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca, na ārohapariṇāhasampanno.

And how, bhikkhus, is a wretched person endowed with speed and with beauty, but not with height and girth? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu understands as it truly is: ‘This is suffering’ … ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ This, I say, is his speed. And when asked a question on the Abhidhamma and the Abhivinaya, he answers and does not falter. This, I say, is his beauty. But he is not a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicines and provisions for the sick. This, I say, is not his height and girth. It is in this way, bhikkhus, that a wretched person is endowed with speed and with beauty, but not with height and girth.

“Kathañca[V 3.0209], bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu ‘idaṃ dukkha’nti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti…pe… ‘ayaṃ dukkhanirodhagāminī paṭipadā’ti yathābhūtaṃ pajānāti. Idamassa javasmiṃ vadāmi. Abhidhamme kho pana abhivinaye pañhaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeti, no saṃsādeti. Idamassa vaṇṇasmiṃ vadāmi. Lābhī [P 4.0399] kho pana [M 3.0200] hoti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Idamassa ārohapariṇāhasmiṃ vadāmi. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, purisakhaḷuṅko javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime kho, bhikkhave, tayo purisakhaḷuṅkā.

And how, bhikkhus, is a wretched person endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu understands as it truly is: ‘This is suffering’ … ‘This is the path leading to the cessation of suffering.’ This, I say, is his speed. And when asked a question on the Abhidhamma and the Abhivinaya, he answers and does not falter. This, I say, is his beauty. And he is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicines and provisions for the sick. This, I say, is his height and girth. It is in this way, bhikkhus, that a wretched person is endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth. These, bhikkhus, are the three wretched persons.

“Katame ca, bhikkhave, tayo assaparassā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco assaparasso…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime kho, bhikkhave, tayo assaparassā.

And what, bhikkhus, are the three superior horses? Here, bhikkhus, a certain superior horse is endowed with speed, but not with beauty nor with height and girth; another is endowed with speed and beauty, but not with height and girth; and another is endowed with speed, beauty, and height and girth. These, bhikkhus, are the three superior horses.

“Katame ca, bhikkhave, tayo purisaparassā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco purisaparasso…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca.

And what, bhikkhus, are the three superior persons? Here, bhikkhus, a certain superior person is endowed with speed, but not with beauty nor with height and girth; another is endowed with speed and beauty, but not with height and girth; and another is endowed with speed, beauty, and height and girth.

“Kathañca[T 4.0412], bhikkhave, purisaparasso…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ parikkhayā opapātiko hoti, tattha parinibbāyī anāvattidhammo tasmā lokā. Idamassa javasmiṃ vadāmi. Abhidhamme kho pana abhivinaye pañhaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeti, no saṃsādeti. Idamassa vaṇṇasmiṃ vadāmi. Lābhī kho pana hoti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Idamassa ārohapariṇāhasmiṃ vadāmi. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, purisaparasso javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime kho, bhikkhave, tayo purisaparassā.

And how, Bhikkhus, is a superior person... endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth? Here, Bhikkhus, with the complete destruction of the five lower fetters, a bhikkhu arises through spontaneous rebirth, is one who will attain Parinibbāna there, and is not liable to return from that world. This, I say, is his speed. Moreover, when asked a question on the higher Dhamma and the higher discipline, he answers and does not falter. This, I say, is his beauty. And he is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicines and provisions for the sick. This, I say, is his height and girth. It is in this way, Bhikkhus, that a superior person is endowed with speed, with beauty, and with height and girth. These, Bhikkhus, are the three superior persons.

“Katame ca, bhikkhave, tayo bhaddā assājānīyā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco bhaddo assājānīyo…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime [P 4.0400] kho, bhikkhave, tayo bhaddā assājānīyā.

And what, Bhikkhus, are the three excellent thoroughbred horses? Here, Bhikkhus, a certain excellent thoroughbred horse... is endowed with speed, endowed with beauty, and endowed with height and girth. These, Bhikkhus, are the three excellent thoroughbred horses.

“Katame [V 3.0210] ca, bhikkhave, tayo bhaddā purisājānīyā? Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco bhaddo purisājānīyo…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca.

And what, Bhikkhus, are the three fine thoroughbred persons? Here, Bhikkhus, a certain fine thoroughbred person... is endowed with speed, endowed with beauty, and endowed with stature and build.

“Kathañca, bhikkhave, bhaddo purisājānīyo…pe… javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu āsavānaṃ khayā anāsavaṃ cetovimuttiṃ paññāvimuttiṃ diṭṭheva [T 4.0413] dhamme sayaṃ abhiññā [M 3.0201] sacchikatvā upasampajja viharati. Idamassa javasmiṃ vadāmi. Abhidhamme kho pana abhivinaye pañhaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeti, no saṃsādeti. Idamassa vaṇṇasmiṃ vadāmi. Lābhī kho pana hoti cīvarapiṇḍapātasenāsanagilānapaccayabhesajjaparikkhārānaṃ. Idamassa ārohapariṇāhasmiṃ vadāmi. Evaṃ kho, bhikkhave, bhaddo purisājānīyo javasampanno ca hoti vaṇṇasampanno ca ārohapariṇāhasampanno ca. Ime kho, bhikkhave, tayo bhaddā purisājānīyā”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

And how, Bhikkhus, is a fine thoroughbred person... endowed with speed, endowed with beauty, and endowed with stature and build? Here, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu, through the destruction of the taints, having realized for himself with direct knowledge, in this very life, the taintless liberation of mind, liberation by wisdom, enters and dwells in it. This, I say, is his speed. Moreover, when asked a question on the higher Dhamma and the higher discipline, he answers and does not falter. This, I say, is his beauty. And he is a recipient of robes, almsfood, lodgings, and medicinal requisites for the sick. This, I say, is his stature and build. Thus, Bhikkhus, a fine thoroughbred person is endowed with speed, endowed with beauty, and endowed with stature and build. These, Bhikkhus, are the three fine thoroughbred persons.

3. Taṇhāmūlakasuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on What is Rooted in Craving

23. dī. ni. 2.103 “Nava, bhikkhave, taṇhāmūlake dhamme desessāmi, taṃ suṇātha. Katame ca, bhikkhave, nava taṇhāmūlakā dhammā? Taṇhaṃ paṭicca pariyesanā, pariyesanaṃ paṭicca lābho, lābhaṃ paṭicca vinicchayo, vinicchayaṃ paṭicca chandarāgo, chandarāgaṃ paṭicca ajjhosānaṃ, ajjhosānaṃ paṭicca pariggaho, pariggahaṃ paṭicca macchariyaṃ, macchariyaṃ paṭicca ārakkho, ārakkhādhikaraṇaṃ daṇḍādānaṃ satthādānaṃ [P 4.0401] kalahaviggahavivādatuvaṃtuvaṃpesuññamusāvādā aneke pāpakā akusalā dhammā sambhavanti. Ime kho, bhikkhave, nava taṇhāmūlakā dhammā”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

23. Bhikkhus, I will teach you nine phenomena rooted in craving. Listen to that. And what, Bhikkhus, are the nine phenomena rooted in craving? Dependent on craving, seeking comes to be. Dependent on seeking, acquisition comes to be. Dependent on acquisition, decision-making comes to be. Dependent on decision-making, desire and lust come to be.
