So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, aparena samayena rūpesu ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulamakāsiṃ, ākāsānañcāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseviṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, ākāsānañcāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā [M 3.0238] paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharāmi. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, iminā vihārena viharato rūpasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho. Seyyathāpi, ānanda, sukhino dukkhaṃ uppajjeyya yāvadeva ābādhāya; evamevassa me rūpasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho.

Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What if, by completely transcending perceptions of form, with the cessation of perceptions of resistance, and by not attending to perceptions of diversity, aware that “Space is infinite,” I were to enter and dwell in the base of infinite space?’ But, Ānanda, my mind did not leap into the base of infinite space, did not find confidence in it, did not settle upon it, and was not liberated in it, though I saw it as peaceful. Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What is the cause, what is the reason, why my mind does not leap into the base of infinite space, find confidence in it, settle upon it, or become liberated in it, though I see it as peaceful?’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘I have not seen the danger in forms, nor have I made much of it; I have not attained the benefit in the base of infinite space, nor have I cultivated it. That is why my mind does not leap into the base of infinite space, does not find confidence in it, does not settle upon it, and is not liberated in it, though I see it as peaceful.’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘If I were to see the danger in forms and make much of it, and attain the benefit in the base of infinite space and cultivate it, it is possible that my mind would leap into the base of infinite space, find confidence in it, settle upon it, and be liberated in it, seeing it as peaceful.’ So, Ānanda, on a later occasion, having seen the danger in forms, I made much of it; having attained the benefit in the base of infinite space, I cultivated it. Then, Ānanda, my mind leaps into the base of infinite space, finds confidence in it, settles upon it, and is liberated in it, seeing it as peaceful. So indeed, Ānanda, by completely transcending perceptions of form, with the cessation of perceptions of resistance, and by not attending to perceptions of diversity, aware that ‘Space is infinite,’ I enter and dwell in the base of infinite space. While I am dwelling in this attainment, Ānanda, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by form arise in me. That is an affliction for me. Just as, Ānanda, pain might arise for a happy person solely as an affliction, in the same way, those perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by form arise as an affliction for me.

“Tassa [T 4.0464] mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘yaṃnūnāhaṃ sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma [V 3.0248] “anantaṃ viññāṇa”nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja vihareyya’nti. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, viññāṇañcāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘ko nu kho hetu ko paccayo, yena me viññāṇañcāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’? Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘ākāsānañcāyatane kho me ādīnavo adiṭṭho, so ca abahulīkato, viññāṇañcāyatane ca ānisaṃso anadhigato, so ca me anāsevito. Tasmā me viññāṇañcāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ ākāsānañcāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulaṃ [P 4.0445] kareyyaṃ, viññāṇañcāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseveyyaṃ, ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me viññāṇañcāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandeyya pasīdeyya santiṭṭheyya vimucceyya etaṃ santanti passato’. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, aparena samayena ākāsānañcāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulamakāsiṃ, viññāṇañcāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseviṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, viññāṇañcāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ [T 4.0465] upasampajja viharāmi. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, iminā vihārena viharato ākāsānañcāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho. Seyyathāpi, ānanda, sukhino dukkhaṃ uppajjeyya yāvadeva ābādhāya[M 3.0239]; evamevassa me ākāsānañcāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho.

Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What if, by completely transcending the base of infinite space, aware that “Consciousness is infinite,” I were to enter and dwell in the base of infinite consciousness?’ But, Ānanda, my mind did not leap into the base of infinite consciousness, did not find confidence in it, did not settle upon it, and was not liberated in it, though I saw it as peaceful. Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What is the cause, what is the reason, why my mind does not leap into the base of infinite consciousness, find confidence in it, settle upon it, or become liberated in it, though I see it as peaceful?’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘I have not seen the danger in the base of infinite space, nor have I made much of it; I have not attained the benefit in the base of infinite consciousness, nor have I cultivated it. That is why my mind does not leap into the base of infinite consciousness, does not find confidence in it, does not settle upon it, and is not liberated in it, though I see it as peaceful.’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘If I were to see the danger in the base of infinite space and make much of it, and attain the benefit in the base of infinite consciousness and cultivate it, it is possible that my mind would leap into the base of infinite consciousness, find confidence in it, settle upon it, and be liberated in it, seeing it as peaceful.’ So, Ānanda, on a later occasion, having seen the danger in the base of infinite space, I made much of it; having attained the benefit in the base of infinite consciousness, I cultivated it. Then, Ānanda, my mind leaps into the base of infinite consciousness, finds confidence in it, settles upon it, and is liberated in it, seeing it as peaceful. So indeed, Ānanda, by completely transcending the base of infinite space, aware that ‘Consciousness is infinite,’ I enter and dwell in the base of infinite consciousness. While I am dwelling in this attainment, Ānanda, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of infinite space arise in me. That is an affliction for me. Just as, Ānanda, pain might arise for a happy person solely as an affliction, in the same way, those perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of infinite space arise as an affliction for me.

“Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘yaṃnūnāhaṃ sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma natthi kiñcīti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja vihareyya’nti. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, ākiñcaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘ko nu kho hetu ko paccayo, yena me ākiñcaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’? Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘viññāṇañcāyatane kho me ādīnavo adiṭṭho, so ca me abahulīkato, ākiñcaññāyatane ca ānisaṃso anadhigato, so ca me anāsevito. Tasmā me ākiñcaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati [V 3.0249] na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ viññāṇañcāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulaṃ kareyyaṃ, ākiñcaññāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseveyyaṃ, ṭhānaṃ kho [T 4.0466] panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me ākiñcaññāyatane [P 4.0446] cittaṃ pakkhandeyya pasīdeyya santiṭṭheyya vimucceyya etaṃ santanti passato’. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, aparena samayena viññāṇañcāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulamakāsiṃ, ākiñcaññāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseviṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, ākiñcaññāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharāmi. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, iminā vihārena viharato viññāṇañcāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho. Seyyathāpi, ānanda, sukhino dukkhaṃ uppajjeyya yāvadeva ābādhāya; evamevassa me viññāṇañcāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho.

Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘Suppose that by completely surmounting the base of the infinity of consciousness, I might enter and dwell in the base of nothingness, aware that “there is nothing.”’ But my mind did not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of nothingness, when I saw it as peaceful. Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What is the cause, what is the condition, why my mind does not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of nothingness, when I see it as peaceful?’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘I have not seen the danger in the base of the infinity of consciousness, nor have I made much of it. And I have not attained the benefit in the base of nothingness, nor have I cultivated it. That is why my mind does not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of nothingness, when I see it as peaceful.’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘If I were to see the danger in the base of the infinity of consciousness and make much of it, and if I were to attain the benefit in the base of nothingness and cultivate it, then it is possible that my mind would enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, and become liberated in the base of nothingness, when I see it as peaceful.’ So then, Ānanda, on a later occasion, I saw the danger in the base of the infinity of consciousness and made much of it, and I attained the benefit in the base of nothingness and cultivated it. Then, Ānanda, my mind entered into, became tranquil in, became steady in, and became liberated in the base of nothingness, when I saw it as peaceful. Thus, Ānanda, by completely surmounting the base of the infinity of consciousness, I entered and dwelled in the base of nothingness, aware that ‘there is nothing.’ But while I was dwelling with this attainment, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of the infinity of consciousness arose in me. This was an affliction for me. Just as, Ānanda, pain might arise for a person who is happy, serving only as an affliction, so too, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of the infinity of consciousness arose in me. This was an affliction for me.

“Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘yaṃnūnāhaṃ sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja vihareyya’nti. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, nevasaññānāsaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi [M 3.0240] – ‘ko nu kho hetu ko paccayo, yena me nevasaññānāsaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’? Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘ākiñcaññāyatane kho me ādīnavo adiṭṭho, so ca me abahulīkato, nevasaññānāsaññāyatane [T 4.0467] ca ānisaṃso anadhigato, so ca me anāsevito. Tasmā me nevasaññānāsaññāyatane cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ ākiñcaññāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulaṃ kareyyaṃ, nevasaññānāsaññāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseveyyaṃ, ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me nevasaññānāsaññāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandeyya pasīdeyya santiṭṭheyya vimucceyya etaṃ santanti passato’. So [P 4.0447] kho ahaṃ, ānanda, aparena samayena ākiñcaññāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulamakāsiṃ, nevasaññānāsaññāyatane ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseviṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, nevasaññānāsaññāyatane cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati etaṃ santanti [V 3.0250] passato. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharāmi. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, iminā vihārena viharato ākiñcaññāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho. Seyyathāpi, ānanda, sukhino dukkhaṃ uppajjeyya yāvadeva ābādhāya; evamevassa me ākiñcaññāyatanasahagatā saññāmanasikārā samudācaranti. Svassa me hoti ābādho.

Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘Suppose that by completely surmounting the base of nothingness, I might enter and dwell in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception.’ But my mind did not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, when I saw it as peaceful. Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘What is the cause, what is the condition, why my mind does not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, when I see it as peaceful?’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘I have not seen the danger in the base of nothingness, nor have I made much of it. And I have not attained the benefit in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, nor have I cultivated it. That is why my mind does not enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, or become liberated in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, when I see it as peaceful.’ Then, Ānanda, it occurred to me: ‘If I were to see the danger in the base of nothingness and make much of it, and if I were to attain the benefit in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception and cultivate it, then it is possible that my mind would enter into, become tranquil in, become steady in, and become liberated in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, when I see it as peaceful.’ So then, Ānanda, on a later occasion, I saw the danger in the base of nothingness and made much of it, and I attained the benefit in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception and cultivated it. Then, Ānanda, my mind entered into, became tranquil in, became steady in, and became liberated in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, when I saw it as peaceful. Thus, Ānanda, by completely surmounting the base of nothingness, I entered and dwelled in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception. But while I was dwelling with this attainment, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of nothingness arose in me. This was an affliction for me. Just as, Ānanda, pain might arise for a person who is happy, serving only as an affliction, so too, perceptions and instances of attention accompanied by the base of nothingness arose in me. This was an affliction for me.

“Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘yaṃnūnāhaṃ nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja vihareyya’nti. Tassa [T 4.0468] mayhaṃ, ānanda, saññāvedayitanirodhe cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘ko nu kho hetu, ko paccayo, yena me saññāvedayitanirodhe cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’? Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘nevasaññānāsaññāyatane kho me ādīnavo adiṭṭho, so ca me abahulīkato, saññāvedayitanirodhe ca ānisaṃso anadhigato, so [M 3.0241] ca me anāsevito. Tasmā me saññāvedayitanirodhe cittaṃ na pakkhandati nappasīdati na santiṭṭhati na vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato’. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, etadahosi – ‘sace kho ahaṃ nevasaññānāsaññāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulaṃ kareyyaṃ, saññāvedayitanirodhe ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseveyyaṃ, ṭhānaṃ kho panetaṃ vijjati yaṃ me saññāvedayitanirodhe cittaṃ pakkhandeyya pasīdeyya santiṭṭheyya vimucceyya etaṃ santanti passato’. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, aparena samayena nevasaññānāsaññāyatane ādīnavaṃ disvā taṃ bahulamakāsiṃ[P 4.0448], saññāvedayitanirodhe ānisaṃsaṃ adhigamma tamāseviṃ. Tassa mayhaṃ, ānanda, saññāvedayitanirodhe cittaṃ pakkhandati pasīdati santiṭṭhati vimuccati etaṃ santanti passato. So kho ahaṃ, ānanda, sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharāmi, paññāya ca me disvā āsavā parikkhayaṃ agamaṃsu.

Then, Ānanda, this thought occurred to me: ‘It would be good if I, having completely transcended the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, might attain and dwell in the cessation of perception and feeling.’ But, Ānanda, though seeing it as peaceful, my mind did not leap up to, become confident in, settle upon, or find release in the cessation of perception and feeling. Then, Ānanda, this thought occurred to me: ‘What is the cause, what is the reason, why my mind does not leap up to, become confident in, settle upon, or find release in the cessation of perception and feeling, though I see it as peaceful?’ Then, Ānanda, this thought occurred to me: ‘The drawback in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception has not been seen by me, and it has not been made much of by me; and the benefit in the cessation of perception and feeling has not been attained by me, and it has not been cultivated by me. That is why my mind does not leap up to, become confident in, settle upon, or find release in the cessation of perception and feeling, though I see it as peaceful.’ Then, Ānanda, this thought occurred to me: ‘If, having seen the drawback in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, I were to make much of it, and if, having attained the benefit in the cessation of perception and feeling, I were to cultivate it, it is possible that my mind would leap up to, become confident in, settle upon, and find release in the cessation of perception and feeling, seeing it as peaceful.’ So, Ānanda, at a later time, having seen the drawback in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, I made much of it; and having attained the benefit in the cessation of perception and feeling, I cultivated it. Then, Ānanda, my mind leaps up to, becomes confident in, settles upon, and finds release in the cessation of perception and feeling, seeing it as peaceful. So, Ānanda, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, I attain and dwell in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, my taints were utterly destroyed.

“Yāvakīvañcāhaṃ[T 4.0469], ānanda, imā nava anupubbavihārasamāpattiyo na evaṃ anulomapaṭilomaṃ samāpajjimpi vuṭṭhahimpi, neva tāvāhaṃ, ānanda, sadevake loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya ‘anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho’ti paccaññāsiṃ. Yato ca kho ahaṃ, ānanda, imā nava anupubbavihārasamāpattiyo evaṃ anulomapaṭilomaṃ [V 3.0251] samāpajjimpi vuṭṭhahimpi, athāhaṃ, ānanda, sadevake loke samārake sabrahmake sassamaṇabrāhmaṇiyā pajāya sadevamanussāya ‘anuttaraṃ sammāsambodhiṃ abhisambuddho’ti paccaññāsiṃ. Ñāṇañca pana me dassanaṃ udapādi – ‘akuppā me cetovimutti vimutti (ka. sī. ka.), ayamantimā jāti, natthi dāni punabbhavo’”ti. Dasamaṃ.

Ānanda, for as long as I did not enter upon and emerge from these nine successive dwelling-attainments in this forward and reverse order, for so long, Ānanda, I did not claim to have awakened to the unsurpassed perfect enlightenment in this world with its devas, Māra, and Brahmā, in this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans. But when, Ānanda, I did enter upon and emerge from these nine successive dwelling-attainments in this forward and reverse order, then, Ānanda, I claimed to have awakened to the unsurpassed perfect enlightenment in this world with its devas, Māra, and Brahmā, in this generation with its ascetics and brahmins, with its devas and humans. And knowledge and vision arose in me: ‘My liberation of mind is unshakable; this is the final birth; now there is no more renewed existence.’” The tenth.

Mahāvaggo catuttho.

The Fourth Great Chapter.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary of that:

Dve vihārā ca nibbānaṃ, gāvī jhānena pañcamaṃ;

Ānando brāhmaṇā devo, nāgena tapussena cāti.

Two on Abodes and Nibbāna, the Cow, the fifth with Jhāna; Ānanda, the Brahmins, the Deva, with the Nāga, and with Tapussa.

5. Sāmaññavaggo

5. The Chapter on the State of a Recluse

1. Sambādhasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on Confinement

42. Ekaṃ [M 3.0242][V 3.0252][P 4.0449] samayaṃ āyasmā ānando kosambiyaṃ viharati ghositārāme. Atha kho āyasmā udāyī yenāyasmā ānando tenupasaṅkami; upasaṅkamitvā āyasmatā ānandena saddhiṃ sammodi. Sammodanīyaṃ kathaṃ sāraṇīyaṃ vītisāretvā ekamantaṃ nisīdi. Ekamantaṃ nisinno [T 4.0470] kho āyasmā udāyī āyasmantaṃ ānandaṃ etadavoca – “vuttamidaṃ, āvuso, pañcālacaṇḍena devaputtena –

42. At one time the Venerable Ānanda was dwelling at Kosambī in Ghosita’s Park. Then the Venerable Udāyī went to the Venerable Ānanda and exchanged greetings with him. When this courteous and amiable talk was finished, he sat down to one side. Seated to one side, the Venerable Udāyī said to the Venerable Ānanda: “Friend, this was said by the devaputta Pañcālacaṇḍa:

“Sambādhe gataṃ sambādhe vata (sī.) okāsaṃ, avidvā bhūrimedhaso;

Yo jhānamabujjhi buddho, paṭilīnanisabho munī”ti.

“He found an opening in confinement, the one of vast wisdom; the Buddha who awakened to jhāna, the secluded bull, the sage.”

“Katamo, āvuso, sambādho, katamo sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā”ti? “Pañcime, āvuso, kāmaguṇā sambādho vutto bhagavatā. Katame pañca? Cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā, sotaviññeyyā saddā…pe… ghānaviññeyyā gandhā… jivhāviññeyyā rasā… kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā. Ime kho, āvuso, pañca kāmaguṇā sambādho vutto bhagavatā.

“Friend, what is the confinement, and what is the attainment of an opening in confinement spoken of by the Blessed One?” “Friend, these five cords of sensual pleasure have been called confinement by the Blessed One. What five? Forms cognizable by the eye that are desirable, lovely, agreeable, pleasing, connected with sensual desire, and enticing; sounds cognizable by the ear… odors cognizable by the nose… tastes cognizable by the tongue… tangibles cognizable by the body that are desirable, lovely, agreeable, pleasing, connected with sensual desire, and enticing. These, friend, are the five cords of sensual pleasure that have been called confinement by the Blessed One.

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva [P 4.0450] tattha vitakkavicārā aniruddhā honti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures… enters and dwells in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that thinking and examining have not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā…pe… dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena[V 3.0253]. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha pīti aniruddhā hoti[T 4.0471], ayamettha sambādho.

“Again, friend, with the subsiding of thinking and examining… a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the second jhāna. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that rapture has not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna [M 3.0243] caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu pītiyā ca virāgā…pe… tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha upekkhāsukhaṃ aniruddhaṃ hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Again, friend, with the fading away of rapture… a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the third jhāna. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the equanimous happiness has not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sukhassa ca pahānā…pe… catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha rūpasaññā aniruddhā hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Furthermore, friend, with the abandoning of pleasure… a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the fourth jhāna. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the perception of form has not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha ākāsānañcāyatanasaññā aniruddhā hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Furthermore, friend, with the complete transcendence of perceptions of form, with the passing away of perceptions of resistance, with non-attention to perceptions of diversity, aware that ‘space is infinite,’ a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the base of the infinity of space. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the perception of the base of the infinity of space has not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘anantaṃ viññāṇa’nti viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ [P 4.0451] upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena[T 4.0472]. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha viññāṇañcāyatanasaññā aniruddhā hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Furthermore, friend, by completely transcending the base of the infinity of space, aware that ‘consciousness is infinite,’ a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the base of the infinity of consciousness. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the perception of the base of the infinity of consciousness has not ceased—this is the confinement here.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ samatikkamma ‘natthi kiñcī’ti ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo [V 3.0254] vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha ākiñcaññāyatanasaññā aniruddhā hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Furthermore, friend, by completely transcending the base of the infinity of consciousness, aware that ‘there is nothing,’ a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the base of nothingness. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the perception of the base of nothingness has not ceased—this is the confinement here.”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena. Tatrāpatthi [M 3.0244] sambādho. Kiñca tattha sambādho? Yadeva tattha nevasaññānāsaññāyatanasaññā aniruddhā hoti, ayamettha sambādho.

“Furthermore, friend, by completely transcending the base of nothingness, a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a provisional sense. And there is still confinement here. What is the confinement here? Just that the perception of the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception has not ceased—this is the confinement here.”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sambādhe okāsādhigamo vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, a bhikkhu enters and dwells in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. To this extent, friend, an attainment of an opening in confinement has been spoken of by the Blessed One in a definitive sense.” The first.

2. Kāyasakkhīsuttaṃ

2. The Discourse on the Body-Witness

43. “‘Kāyasakkhī kāyasakkhī’ti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, kāyasakkhī vutto bhagavatā”ti? “Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati. Ettāvatāpi [T 4.0473] kho, āvuso, kāyasakkhī vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena.

43. “‘Body-witness, body-witness,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has a body-witness been declared by the Blessed One?” “Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures …pe… enters and dwells in the first jhāna. In whatever way that base exists, he dwells having contacted it with the mental body. To this extent, friend, a body-witness has been declared by the Blessed One in a provisional sense.

“Puna [P 4.0452] caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu vitakkavicārānaṃ vūpasamā…pe… dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ… tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ… catutthaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, kāyasakkhī vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, with the stilling of directed thought and evaluation …pe… enters and dwells in the second jhāna … the third jhāna … the fourth jhāna. In whatever way that base exists, he dwells having contacted it with the mental body. To this extent, friend, a body-witness has been declared by the Blessed One in a provisional sense.

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso rūpasaññānaṃ samatikkamā paṭighasaññānaṃ atthaṅgamā [V 3.0255] nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā ‘ananto ākāso’ti ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ upasampajja viharati. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, kāyasakkhī vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, with the complete surmounting of perceptions of form, with the passing away of perceptions of resistance, with non-attention to perceptions of diversity, aware that ‘space is infinite,’ enters and dwells in the base of infinite space. In whatever way that base exists, he dwells having contacted it with the mental body. To this extent, friend, a body-witness has been declared by the Blessed One in a provisional sense …pe….

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, kāyasakkhī vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely surmounting the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and dwells in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. In whatever way that base exists, he dwells having contacted it with the mental body. To this extent, friend, a body-witness has been declared by the Blessed One in a definitive sense.” Second.

3. Paññāvimuttasuttaṃ

3. The Discourse on One Liberated by Wisdom

44. “‘Paññāvimutto [M 3.0245] paññāvimutto’ti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, paññāvimutto vutto bhagavatā”ti?

44. “‘Liberated by wisdom, liberated by wisdom,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has one who is liberated by wisdom been declared by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya ca naṃ pajānāti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, paññāvimutto vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures …pe… enters and dwells in the first jhāna, and he understands it with wisdom. To this extent, friend, one who is liberated by wisdom has been declared by the Blessed One in a provisional sense …pe….”

“Puna [T 4.0474][P 4.0453] caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti, paññāya ca naṃ pajānāti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, paññāvimutto vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely surmounting the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and dwells in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed, and he understands this with wisdom. To this extent, friend, one who is liberated by wisdom has been declared by the Blessed One in a definitive sense.” Third.

4. Ubhatobhāgavimuttasuttaṃ

4. The Discourse on One Liberated in Both Ways

45. “‘Ubhatobhāgavimutto [V 3.0256] ubhatobhāgavimutto’ti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, ubhatobhāgavimutto vutto bhagavatā”ti?

45. “‘Liberated in both ways, liberated in both ways,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has one who is liberated in both ways been declared by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati, paññāya ca naṃ pajānāti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, ubhatobhāgavimutto vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures …pe… enters and dwells in the first jhāna. And in whatever way that base is, in that same way he dwells having contacted it with the mental body, and he understands it with wisdom. To this extent, friend, one who is liberated in both ways has been declared by the Blessed One indirectly …pe….”

“Puna [T 4.0475] caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Yathā yathā ca tadāyatanaṃ tathā tathā naṃ kāyena phusitvā viharati, paññāya ca naṃ pajānāti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, ubhatobhāgavimutto vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely surmounting the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and dwells in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. And in whatever way that base is, in that same way he dwells having contacted it with the mental body, and he understands it with wisdom. To this extent, friend, one who is liberated in both ways has been declared by the Blessed One directly.” Fourth.

5. Sandiṭṭhikadhammasuttaṃ

5. The Discourse on the Dhamma Visible Here and Now

46. “‘Sandiṭṭhiko dhammo sandiṭṭhiko dhammo’ti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhiko dhammo vutto bhagavatā”ti?

46. “‘The Dhamma is visible here and now, the Dhamma is visible here and now,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has the Dhamma been declared by the Blessed One to be visible here and now?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi [M 3.0246] kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhiko dhammo vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, quite secluded from sensual pleasures …pe… enters and dwells in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend, the Dhamma has been declared by the Blessed One to be visible here and now indirectly …pe….”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhiko dhammo vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely surmounting the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and dwells in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. To this extent, friend, the Dhamma has been declared by the Blessed One to be visible here and now directly.” Fifth.

6. Sandiṭṭhikanibbānasuttaṃ

6. The Discourse on Nibbāna Visible Here and Now

47. “‘Sandiṭṭhikaṃ [V 3.0257] nibbānaṃ sandiṭṭhikaṃ nibbāna’nti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhikaṃ nibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā”ti?

47. “‘Nibbāna visible here and now, Nibbāna visible here and now,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has Nibbāna visible here and now been spoken of by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhikaṃ nibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, secluded from sensual pleasures …pe… enters and abides in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend, Nibbāna visible here and now has been spoken of by the Blessed One indirectly…pe….”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, sandiṭṭhikaṃ nibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. To this extent, friend, Nibbāna visible here and now has been spoken of by the Blessed One directly.” Sixth.

7. Nibbānasuttaṃ

7. The Discourse on Nibbāna

48. “‘Nibbānaṃ [P 4.0454] nibbāna’nti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Sattamaṃ.

48. “‘Nibbāna, Nibbāna,’ it is said, friend…pe… Seventh.

8. Parinibbānasuttaṃ

8. The Discourse on Parinibbāna

49. “‘Parinibbānaṃ parinibbāna’nti…pe…. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

49. “‘Parinibbāna, Parinibbāna,’ it is said…pe… Eighth.

9. Tadaṅganibbānasuttaṃ

9. The Discourse on Temporary Nibbāna

50. “‘Tadaṅganibbānaṃ [V 3.0258] tadaṅganibbāna’nti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Navamaṃ.

50. “‘Temporary Nibbāna, temporary Nibbāna,’ it is said, friend…pe… Ninth.

10. Diṭṭhadhammanibbānasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on Nibbāna in This Very Life

51. “‘Diṭṭhadhammanibbānaṃ diṭṭhadhammanibbāna’nti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso diṭṭhadhammanibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā”ti?

51. “‘Nibbāna in this very life, Nibbāna in this very life,’ it is said, friend. To what extent, friend, has Nibbāna in this very life been spoken of by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi [M 3.0247] …pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, diṭṭhadhammanibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā pariyāyena [T 4.0476] …pe….

“Here, friend Kāḷudāyī, a bhikkhu, secluded from sensual pleasures…pe… enters and abides in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend Kāḷudāyī, Nibbāna in this very life has been spoken of by the Blessed One indirectly…pe….”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, diṭṭhadhammanibbānaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend Kāḷudāyī, a bhikkhu, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. To this extent, friend Kāḷudāyī, Nibbāna in this very life has been spoken of by the Blessed One directly.” Tenth.

Sāmaññavaggo pañcamo.

The Fifth, the Chapter on the General Theme.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary:

Sambādho [P 4.0455] kāyasakkhī paññā,

Ubhatobhāgo sandiṭṭhikā dve;

Nibbānaṃ parinibbānaṃ,

Tadaṅgadiṭṭhadhammikena cāti.

Confinement, Bodily Witness, Wisdom, Liberated in Both Ways, two on the Visible; Nibbāna, Parinibbāna, Temporary, and with the one in this very life.

Paṭhamapaṇṇāsakaṃ samattaṃ.

The First Fifty is completed.

2. Dutiyapaṇṇāsakaṃ

2. The Second Fifty

(6) 1. Khemavaggo

(6) 1. The Chapter on Security

1. Khemasuttaṃ

The Discourse on Security

52. “‘Khemaṃ [T 4.0477] khema’nti[M 3.0248][V 3.0259], āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā nu kho, āvuso, khemaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā”ti?

1. “‘Security, security,’ it is said, friend Ānanda. To what extent, friend Ānanda, has security been spoken of by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, khemaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe….

52. “Here, friend Kāḷudāyī, a bhikkhu, secluded from sensual pleasures…pe… enters and abides in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend Kāḷudāyī, security has been spoken of by the Blessed One indirectly…pe….”

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, khemaṃ vuttaṃ bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend Kāḷudāyī, a bhikkhu, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. To this extent, friend Kāḷudāyī, security has been spoken of by the Blessed One directly.” First.

2. Khemappattasuttaṃ

The Discourse on Attaining Security

53. Khemappatto khemappattoti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Dutiyaṃ.

2. “‘One who has attained security, one who has attained security,’ it is said, friend…pe… Second.

3. Amatasuttaṃ

53. The Discourse on the Deathless

54. Amataṃ amatanti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Tatiyaṃ.

3. “‘The deathless, the deathless,’ it is said, friend…pe… Third.

4. Amatappattasuttaṃ

54. The Discourse on One Who Has Attained the Deathless

55. Amatappatto amatappattoti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Catutthaṃ.

4. “‘One who has attained the deathless, one who has attained the deathless,’ it is said, friend…pe… Fourth.

5. Abhayasuttaṃ

55. The Discourse on Fearlessness

56. Abhayaṃ [V 3.0260] abhayanti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Pañcamaṃ.

5. “‘Fearlessness, fearlessness,’ it is said, friend…pe… Fifth.

6. Abhayappattasuttaṃ

56. The Discourse on One Who Has Attained Fearlessness

57. Abhayappatto abhayappattoti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

6. “‘One who has attained fearlessness, one who has attained fearlessness,’ it is said, friend…pe… Sixth.

7. Passaddhisuttaṃ

7. The Sutta on Tranquility

58. Passaddhi passaddhīti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Sattamaṃ.

58. “Friend, it is said, ‘tranquility, tranquility.’… The seventh.

8. Anupubbapassaddhisuttaṃ

8. The Sutta on Gradual Tranquility

59. Anupubbapassaddhi [M 3.0249][P 4.0456] anupubbapassaddhīti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

59. “Friend, it is said, ‘gradual tranquility, gradual tranquility.’… The eighth.

9. Nirodhasuttaṃ

9. The Sutta on Cessation

60. Nirodho nirodhoti, āvuso, vuccati…pe…. Navamaṃ.

60. “Friend, it is said, ‘cessation, cessation.’… The ninth.

10. Anupubbanirodhasuttaṃ

10. The Sutta on Gradual Cessation

61. “‘Anupubbanirodho anupubbanirodho’ti, āvuso, vuccati. Kittāvatā [T 4.0478] nu kho, āvuso, anupubbanirodho vutto bhagavatā”ti?

61. “Friend, it is said, ‘gradual cessation, gradual cessation.’ To what extent, friend, has gradual cessation been spoken of by the Blessed One?”

“Idhāvuso, bhikkhu vivicceva kāmehi…pe… paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ upasampajja viharati. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, anupubbanirodho vutto bhagavatā pariyāyena…pe…[V 3.0261].

“Here, friend, a bhikkhu, secluded from sensual pleasures… enters and abides in the first jhāna. To this extent, friend, gradual cessation has been spoken of by the Blessed One by way of an indirect method….

“Puna caparaṃ, āvuso, bhikkhu sabbaso nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ samatikkamma saññāvedayitanirodhaṃ upasampajja viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti. Ettāvatāpi kho, āvuso, anupubbanirodho vutto bhagavatā nippariyāyenā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Furthermore, friend, a bhikkhu, by completely transcending the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, enters and abides in the cessation of perception and feeling. And having seen with wisdom, his taints are utterly destroyed. To this extent, friend, gradual cessation has been spoken of by the Blessed One not by way of an indirect method.” The tenth.

11. Abhabbasuttaṃ

11. The Sutta on Incapability

62. “Nava, bhikkhave, dhamme appahāya abhabbo arahattaṃ sacchikātuṃ. Katame nava? Rāgaṃ, dosaṃ, mohaṃ, kodhaṃ, upanāhaṃ, makkhaṃ, paḷāsaṃ, issaṃ, macchariyaṃ – ime kho, bhikkhave, nava dhamme appahāya abhabbo arahattaṃ sacchikātuṃ.

62. “Bhikkhus, without having abandoned nine things, one is incapable of realizing arahantship. What nine? Lust, hatred, delusion, anger, hostility, denigration, contentiousness, envy, and stinginess. Bhikkhus, without having abandoned these nine things, one is incapable of realizing arahantship.

“Nava, bhikkhave, dhamme pahāya bhabbo arahattaṃ sacchikātuṃ. Katame nava? Rāgaṃ, dosaṃ, mohaṃ, kodhaṃ, upanāhaṃ, makkhaṃ, paḷāsaṃ, issaṃ, macchariyaṃ – ime kho, bhikkhave, nava dhamme pahāya bhabbo arahattaṃ sacchikātu”nti. Ekādasamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, having abandoned nine things, one is capable of realizing arahantship. What nine? Lust, hatred, delusion, anger, hostility, denigration, contentiousness, envy, and stinginess. Bhikkhus, having abandoned these nine things, one is capable of realizing arahantship.” The eleventh.

Khemavaggo paṭhamo.

The First Chapter on Security.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary of this is:

Khemo [P 4.0457] ca amatañceva, abhayaṃ passaddhiyena ca;

Nirodho anupubbo ca, dhammaṃ pahāya bhabbena cāti.

Security and the deathless, fearlessness and tranquility; cessation and the gradual, and being capable by abandoning things.

(7) 2. Satipaṭṭhānavaggo

(7) 2. The Chapter on the Foundations of Mindfulness

1. Sikkhādubbalyasuttaṃ

1. The Sutta on Weaknesses in the Training

63. “Pañcimāni[T 4.0479][M 3.0250][V 3.0262], bhikkhave, sikkhādubbalyāni. Katamāni pañca? Pāṇātipāto, adinnādānaṃ, kāmesumicchācāro, musāvādo, surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañca sikkhādubbalyāni.

63. “Bhikkhus, there are these five weaknesses in the training. What five? The destruction of life, the taking of what is not given, misconduct in sensual pleasures, false speech, and indulgence in liquor, wine, and intoxicants, which are a basis for heedlessness. Indeed, bhikkhus, these are the five weaknesses in the training.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ; vedanāsu…pe… citte…pe… dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, the four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu dwells contemplating the body in the body, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world; he dwells contemplating feelings in feelings… he dwells contemplating the mind in the mind… he dwells contemplating phenomena in phenomena, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, these four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed.” The first.

2. Nīvaraṇasuttaṃ

2. The Sutta on the Hindrances

64. “Pañcimāni, bhikkhave, nīvaraṇāni. Katamāni pañca? Kāmacchandanīvaraṇaṃ, byāpādanīvaraṇaṃ, thinamiddhanīvaraṇaṃ[P 4.0458], uddhaccakukkuccanīvaraṇaṃ, vicikicchānīvaraṇaṃ – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañca nīvaraṇāni.

64. “Bhikkhus, there are these five hindrances. What five? The hindrance of sensual desire, the hindrance of ill will, the hindrance of sloth and torpor, the hindrance of restlessness and remorse, and the hindrance of doubt. Indeed, bhikkhus, these are the five hindrances.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ nīvaraṇānaṃ pahānāya cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ; vedanāsu…pe… citte…pe… [V 3.0263] dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ. Imesaṃ [T 4.0480] kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ nīvaraṇānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Dutiyaṃ.

“Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five hindrances, the four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu dwells contemplating the body in the body, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world; he dwells contemplating feelings in feelings… he dwells contemplating the mind in the mind… he dwells contemplating phenomena in phenomena, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five hindrances, these four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed.” The second.

3. Kāmaguṇasuttaṃ

3. The Sutta on the Strands of Sensual Pleasure

65. “Pañcime, bhikkhave, kāmaguṇā. Katame pañca? Cakkhuviññeyyā rūpā iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā, sotaviññeyyā saddā…pe… ghānaviññeyyā gandhā… jivhāviññeyyā rasā… kāyaviññeyyā phoṭṭhabbā [M 3.0251] iṭṭhā kantā manāpā piyarūpā kāmūpasaṃhitā rajanīyā. Ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca kāmaguṇā.

65. “Bhikkhus, there are these five strands of sensual pleasure. What five? Forms cognizable by the eye that are desirable, pleasing, delightful, and endearing, connected with sensual desire, and arousing passion. Sounds cognizable by the ear… smells cognizable by the nose… tastes cognizable by the tongue… tangible objects cognizable by the body that are desirable, pleasing, delightful, and endearing, connected with sensual desire, and arousing passion. Indeed, bhikkhus, these are the five strands of sensual pleasure.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ kāmaguṇānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Tatiyaṃ.

“Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five strands of sensual pleasure… these four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed.” The third.

4. Upādānakkhandhasuttaṃ

4. The Sutta on the Aggregates Subject to Clinging

66. “Pañcime, bhikkhave, upādānakkhandhā. Katame pañca? Rūpupādānakkhandho, vedanupādānakkhandho, saññupādānakkhandho, saṅkhārupādānakkhandho, viññāṇupādānakkhandho – ime [P 4.0459] kho, bhikkhave, pañcupādānakkhandhā.

66. “Bhikkhus, there are these five aggregates subject to clinging. What five? The form aggregate subject to clinging, the feeling aggregate subject to clinging, the perception aggregate subject to clinging, the formations aggregate subject to clinging, and the consciousness aggregate subject to clinging. These, bhikkhus, are the five aggregates subject to clinging.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ upādānakkhandhānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Catutthaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five aggregates subject to clinging… these four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed.” The fourth.

5. Orambhāgiyasuttaṃ

5. The Sutta on the Lower Fetters

67. “Pañcimāni, bhikkhave, orambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni. Katamāni pañca? Sakkāyadiṭṭhi, vicikicchā, sīlabbataparāmāso, kāmacchando, byāpādo – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañcorambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni.

67. “Bhikkhus, there are these five lower fetters. What five? Identity view, doubt, clinging to precepts and practices, sensual desire, and ill will. These, bhikkhus, are the five lower fetters.”

“Imesaṃ [V 3.0264] kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ orambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Pañcamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five lower fetters… these four foundations of mindfulness are to be developed.” The fifth.

6. Gatisuttaṃ

6. The Sutta on Destinations

68. “Pañcimā, bhikkhave, gatiyo. Katamā pañca? Nirayo, tiracchānayoni[T 4.0481], pettivisayo, manussā, devā – imā kho, bhikkhave, pañca gatiyo.

68. “Bhikkhus, there are these five destinations. What five? Hell, the animal realm, the sphere of departed spirits, human beings, and gods. These, bhikkhus, are the five destinations.”

“Imāsaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ gatīnaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Chaṭṭhaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five destinations… these four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed.” Sixth.

7. Macchariyasuttaṃ

7. Discourse on Stinginess

69. “Pañcimāni, bhikkhave, macchariyāni. Katamāni pañca? Āvāsamacchariyaṃ, kulamacchariyaṃ, lābhamacchariyaṃ, vaṇṇamacchariyaṃ, dhammamacchariyaṃ – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañca macchariyāni.

69. “Bhikkhus, there are these five kinds of stinginess. What five? Stinginess regarding dwellings, stinginess regarding supporter families, stinginess regarding gains, stinginess regarding beauty, and stinginess regarding the Dhamma. These, bhikkhus, are the five kinds of stinginess.”

“Imesaṃ [M 3.0252] kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ macchariyānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Sattamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five kinds of stinginess… these four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed.” Seventh.

8. Uddhambhāgiyasuttaṃ

8. Discourse on the Higher Fetters

70. “Pañcimāni[P 4.0460], bhikkhave, uddhambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni. Katamāni pañca? Rūparāgo, arūparāgo, māno, uddhaccaṃ, avijjā – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañcuddhambhāgiyāni saṃyojanāni.

70. “Bhikkhus, there are these five higher fetters. What five? Lust for form, lust for the formless, conceit, restlessness, and ignorance. These, bhikkhus, are the five higher fetters.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ uddhambhāgiyānaṃ saṃyojanānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Aṭṭhamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five higher fetters… these four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed.” Eighth.

9. Cetokhilasuttaṃ

9. Discourse on Mental Barrenness

71. a. ni. 5.205; dī. ni. 3.319; ma. ni. 1.185 “Pañcime[V 3.0265], bhikkhave, cetokhilā cetokhīlā (ka.). Katame pañca? Idha bhikkhave, bhikkhu satthari kaṅkhati vicikicchati nādhimuccati na sampasīdati. Yo so, bhikkhave, bhikkhu satthari kaṅkhati vicikicchati nādhimuccati na sampasīdati, tassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya. Yassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya[T 4.0482], ayaṃ paṭhamo cetokhilo.

71. “Bhikkhus, there are these five states of mental barrenness. What are the five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu doubts the Teacher, is uncertain, is not resolved, and has no confidence in him. When a bhikkhu doubts the Teacher, is uncertain, is not resolved, and has no confidence in him, his mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving. When one's mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving, this is the first state of mental barrenness.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu dhamme kaṅkhati…pe… saṅghe kaṅkhati… sikkhāya kaṅkhati… sabrahmacārīsu kupito hoti anattamano āhatacitto khilajāto. Yo so, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sabrahmacārīsu kupito hoti anattamano āhatacitto khilajāto, tassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya. Yassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya, ayaṃ pañcamo cetokhilo.

“Again, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu doubts the Dhamma… doubts the Saṅgha… doubts the training… is angry and displeased with his companions in the holy life, his heart is struck, and he is barren-minded. That bhikkhu, bhikkhus, who is angry and displeased with his companions in the holy life… his mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving. When one's mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving, this is the fifth state of mental barrenness.”

“Imesaṃ, kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetokhilānaṃ pahānāya…pe… ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Navamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five states of mental barrenness… these four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed.” Ninth.

10. Cetasovinibandhasuttaṃ

10. Discourse on the Shackles of the Mind

72. “Pañcime[P 4.0461], bhikkhave, cetasovinibandhā cetovinibaddhā (sāratthadīpanīṭīkā) a. ni. 5.206; dī. ni. 3.320. Katame pañca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāmesu avītarāgo hoti avigatacchando avigatapemo avigatapipāso [M 3.0253] avigatapariḷāho avigatataṇho. Yo so, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāmesu avītarāgo hoti avigatacchando avigatapemo avigatapipāso avigatapariḷāho avigatataṇho, tassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya. Yassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya, ayaṃ paṭhamo cetasovinibandho.

72. “Bhikkhus, there are these five shackles of the mind. What are the five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not devoid of lust for sensual pleasures, not devoid of desire, not devoid of affection, not devoid of thirst, not devoid of fever, not devoid of craving for them. When a bhikkhu is not devoid of lust for sensual pleasures… his mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving. When one's mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving, this is the first shackle of the mind.”

“Puna caparaṃ, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye avītarāgo hoti…pe… rūpe avītarāgo hoti… yāvadatthaṃ udarāvadehakaṃ bhuñjitvā seyyasukhaṃ passasukhaṃ middhasukhaṃ anuyutto viharati [V 3.0266] … aññataraṃ devanikāyaṃ paṇidhāya brahmacariyaṃ carati – ‘imināhaṃ sīlena vā vatena vā tapena vā brahmacariyena vā devo vā bhavissāmi devaññataro [T 4.0483] vā’ti. Yo so, bhikkhave, bhikkhu aññataraṃ devanikāyaṃ paṇidhāya brahmacariyaṃ carati – ‘imināhaṃ sīlena vā vatena vā tapena vā brahmacariyena vā devo vā bhavissāmi devaññataro vā’ti, tassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya. Yassa cittaṃ na namati ātappāya anuyogāya sātaccāya padhānāya, ayaṃ pañcamo cetasovinibandho. Ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca cetasovinibandhā.

“Again, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not devoid of lust for the body… is not devoid of lust for forms… having eaten his fill, as much as his belly can hold, he dwells devoted to the pleasure of lying down, the pleasure of turning from side to side, the pleasure of sleep… he lives the holy life aspiring to be reborn in some order of devas, thinking: ‘By this precept or vow or austerity or holy life, I will become a deva or one of the devas.’ When a bhikkhu lives the holy life aspiring to be reborn in some order of devas… his mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving. When one's mind does not incline to ardor, to application, to perseverance, and to striving, this is the fifth shackle of the mind. These, bhikkhus, are the five shackles of the mind.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāye kāyānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ; vedanāsu…pe… citte…pe… dhammesu dhammānupassī viharati ātāpī sampajāno satimā vineyya loke abhijjhādomanassaṃ. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro satipaṭṭhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five shackles of the mind, the four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu dwells contemplating the body in the body, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. He dwells contemplating feelings in feelings… mind in mind… phenomena in phenomena, ardent, clearly comprehending, and mindful, having removed covetousness and displeasure in regard to the world. For the abandoning of these five shackles of the mind, bhikkhus, these four establishments of mindfulness are to be developed.” Tenth.

Satipaṭṭhānavaggo dutiyo.

The Chapter on the Establishments of Mindfulness, the Second.

Tassuddānaṃ –

The summary:

Sikkhā [P 4.0462] nīvaraṇākāmā, khandhā ca orambhāgiyā gati;

Maccheraṃ uddhambhāgiyā aṭṭhamaṃ, cetokhilā vinibandhāti.

Training, Hindrances, Sensual Pleasures, Aggregates, Lower Fetters, Destinations, Stinginess, Higher Fetters, Mental Barrenness, and Shackles.

(8) 3. Sammappadhānavaggo

(8) 3. The Chapter on the Right Strivings

1. Sikkhasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on the Training

73. “Pañcimāni[M 3.0254][V 3.0267], bhikkhave, sikkhādubbalyāni. Katamāni pañca? Pāṇātipāto [T 4.0484] …pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañca sikkhādubbalyāni.

73. “Bhikkhus, there are these five weaknesses in the training. What five? The destruction of life… indulgence in liquor, wine, and intoxicants, which are a basis for heedlessness. Indeed, bhikkhus, these are the five weaknesses in the training.”

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya cattāro sammappadhānā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu anuppannānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ anuppādāya chandaṃ janeti vāyamati vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati; uppannānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya chandaṃ janeti vāyamati vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati; anuppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ uppādāya chandaṃ janeti vāyamati vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati; uppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ ṭhitiyā asammosāya bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā chandaṃ janeti vāyamati vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro sammappadhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, the four right strivings are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives for the non-arising of unarisen evil, unwholesome states. He generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives for the abandoning of arisen evil, unwholesome states. He generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives for the arising of unarisen wholesome states. He generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives for the maintenance, non-disappearance, greater increase, expansion, development, and fulfillment of arisen wholesome states. Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, these four right strivings are to be developed.”

74-81. (Yathā satipaṭṭhānavagge tathā sammappadhānavasena vitthāretabbā.)

74–81. (To be elaborated by way of the right strivings, as in the chapter on the establishments of mindfulness.)

10. Cetasovinibandhasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Bondages of the Mind

82. “Pañcime[P 4.0463], bhikkhave, cetasovinibandhā. Katame pañca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāmesu avītarāgo hoti…pe… ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca cetasovinibandhā.

82. “Bhikkhus, there are these five bondages of the mind. What five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not devoid of lust for sensual pleasures … Indeed, bhikkhus, these are the five bondages of the mind.

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya cattāro sammappadhānā bhāvetabbā[V 3.0268]. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu anuppannānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ anuppādāya chandaṃ janeti vāyamati [T 4.0485] vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati; uppannānaṃ pāpakānaṃ akusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ pahānāya… anuppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ uppādāya… uppannānaṃ kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ ṭhitiyā asammosāya bhiyyobhāvāya vepullāya bhāvanāya pāripūriyā chandaṃ janeti vāyamati vīriyaṃ ārabhati cittaṃ paggaṇhāti padahati[M 3.0255]. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro sammappadhānā bhāvetabbā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five bondages of the mind, the four right strivings are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives for the non-arising of unarisen evil, unwholesome states; … for the abandoning of arisen evil, unwholesome states; … for the arising of unarisen wholesome states; … for the maintenance, non-disappearance, greater increase, expansion, development, and fulfillment of arisen wholesome states, he generates desire, makes an effort, arouses energy, exerts his mind, and strives. Indeed, bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five bondages of the mind, these four right strivings are to be developed.”

Sammappadhānavaggo tatiyo.

The Third Chapter: The Right Strivings

(9) 4. Iddhipādavaggo

(9) 4. The Chapter on the Bases of Spiritual Power

1. Sikkhasuttaṃ

1. The Discourse on Training

83. “Pañcimāni[V 3.0269], bhikkhave, sikkhādubbalyāni. Katamāni pañca? Pāṇātipāto…pe… surāmerayamajjapamādaṭṭhānaṃ – imāni kho, bhikkhave, pañca sikkhādubbalyāni.

83. “Bhikkhus, there are these five weaknesses in the training. What five? The destruction of life … indulgence in liquor, wine, and intoxicants, which are a basis for heedlessness—these, bhikkhus, are the five weaknesses in the training.

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya cattāro iddhipādā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha[P 4.0464], bhikkhave, bhikkhu chandasamādhipadhānasaṅkhārasamannāgataṃ iddhipādaṃ bhāveti, vīriyasamādhi… cittasamādhi… vīmaṃsāsamādhipadhānasaṅkhārasamannāgataṃ iddhipādaṃ bhāveti. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ sikkhādubbalyānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro iddhipādā bhāvetabbā”ti. Paṭhamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, the four bases of spiritual power are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu develops the basis for spiritual power that possesses concentration due to desire and volitional formations of striving; he develops the basis for spiritual power that possesses concentration due to energy … concentration due to mind … concentration due to investigation and volitional formations of striving. Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five weaknesses in the training, these four bases of spiritual power are to be developed.”

84-91. (Yathā satipaṭṭhānavagge tathā iddhipādavasena vitthāretabbā.)

84–91. (To be elaborated by way of the bases of spiritual power, as in the chapter on the establishments of mindfulness.)

10. Cetasovinibandhasuttaṃ

10. The Discourse on the Bondages of the Mind

92. “Pañcime[T 4.0486], bhikkhave, cetasovinibandhā. Katame pañca? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu kāmesu avītarāgo hoti…pe… ime kho, bhikkhave, pañca cetasovinibandhā.

92. “Bhikkhus, there are these five bondages of the mind. What five? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is not devoid of lust for sensual pleasures … these, bhikkhus, are the five bondages of the mind.

“Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro iddhipādā bhāvetabbā. Katame cattāro? Idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu chandasamādhipadhānasaṅkhārasamannāgataṃ iddhipādaṃ bhāveti, vīriyasamādhi… cittasamādhi… vīmaṃsāsamādhipadhānasaṅkhārasamannāgataṃ iddhipādaṃ [V 3.0270] bhāveti. Imesaṃ kho, bhikkhave, pañcannaṃ cetasovinibandhānaṃ pahānāya ime cattāro iddhipādā bhāvetabbā”ti. Dasamaṃ.

“Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five bondages of the mind, these four bases of spiritual power are to be developed. What four? Here, bhikkhus, a bhikkhu develops the basis for spiritual power that possesses concentration due to desire and volitional formations of striving; he develops the basis for spiritual power that possesses concentration due to energy … concentration due to mind … concentration due to investigation and volitional formations of striving. Bhikkhus, for the abandoning of these five bondages of the mind, these four bases of spiritual power are to be developed.”

Iddhipādavaggo catuttho.

The Fourth Chapter: The Bases of Spiritual Power

Yatheva [M 3.0256] satipaṭṭhānā, padhānā caturopi ca;

Cattāro iddhipādā ca, tatheva sampayojayeti.

Just as the establishments of mindfulness, and also the four right strivings, and the four bases of spiritual power, so too should they be applied.

(10) 5. Rāgapeyyālaṃ

(10) 5. Repetition Series on Lust

93. “Rāgassa[V 3.0271][P 4.0465], bhikkhave, abhiññāya nava dhammā bhāvetabbā. Katame nava? Asubhasaññā, maraṇasaññā, āhāre paṭikūlasaññā, sabbaloke anabhiratasaññā, aniccasaññā, anicce dukkhasaññā, dukkhe anattasaññā, pahānasaññā, virāgasaññā – rāgassa, bhikkhave abhiññāya ime nava dhammā bhāvetabbā”ti.

93. “Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of lust, nine qualities are to be developed. What nine? The perception of foulness, the perception of death, the perception of the repulsiveness of food, the perception of non-delight in the whole world, the perception of impermanence, the perception of suffering in what is impermanent, the perception of not-self in what is suffering, the perception of abandoning, and the perception of dispassion. For the direct knowledge of lust, bhikkhus, these nine qualities are to be developed.”

94. “Rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya nava dhammā bhāvetabbā. Katame [T 4.0487] nava? Paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ, dutiyaṃ jhānaṃ, tatiyaṃ jhānaṃ, catutthaṃ jhānaṃ, ākāsānañcāyatanaṃ, viññāṇañcāyatanaṃ, ākiñcaññāyatanaṃ, nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ, saññāvedayitanirodho – rāgassa, bhikkhave, abhiññāya ime nava dhammā bhāvetabbā”ti.

94. “Bhikkhus, for the direct knowledge of lust, nine qualities are to be developed. What nine? The first jhāna, the second jhāna, the third jhāna, the fourth jhāna, the base of the infinity of space, the base of the infinity of consciousness, the base of nothingness, the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, and the cessation of perception and feeling. For the direct knowledge of lust, bhikkhus, these nine qualities are to be developed.”

95-112. “Rāgassa, bhikkhave, pariññāya…pe… parikkhayāya…pe… pahānāya…pe… khayāya…pe… vayāya…pe… virāgāya…pe… nirodhāya…pe… cāgāya…pe… paṭinissaggāya…pe… ime nava dhammā bhāvetabbā”.

95–112. “Bhikkhus, for the full understanding of lust, … for its complete destruction, … for its abandoning, … for its destruction, … for its passing away, … for its fading away, … for its cessation, … for giving it up, … for its relinquishment, these nine qualities are to be developed.”

113-432. “Dosassa…pe… mohassa… kodhassa… upanāhassa… makkhassa… paḷāsassa… issāya… macchariyassa… māyāya… sāṭheyyassa… thambhassa… sārambhassa… mānassa… atimānassa… madassa… pamādassa abhiññāya…pe… pariññāya… parikkhayāya… pahānāya… khayāya… vayāya… virāgāya… nirodhāya [P 4.0466]… cāgāya… paṭinissaggāya…pe… ime nava dhammā bhāvetabbā”ti.

113–432. “For hatred… delusion… anger… hostility… denigration… competitiveness… envy… stinginess… deceit… fraud… obstinacy… domineering… conceit… excessive conceit… intoxication… and heedlessness; for their direct knowledge… for their full understanding… for their complete destruction… for their abandoning… for their destruction… for their passing away… for their fading away… for their cessation… for giving them up… for their relinquishment, these nine qualities are to be developed.”

Rāgapeyyālaṃ niṭṭhitaṃ.

The Lust Repetition Series is finished.

Navakanipātapāḷi niṭṭhitā.

The Book of the Nines is finished.
